\displaystyle\cdot\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in(L\setminus\widetilde{U})\cup\widetilde{K}}\{v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus\widetilde{K})}(-\infty,v^{(j)}_{m_{j}})\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{K}}(v^{(j)}_{m_{j}},+\infty)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus\widetilde{K})\\
\hskip 8.19447pt\cup((\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\cap S)\end{subarray}}\{v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{|\widetilde{U}\setminus S|}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(f),

which shows that (
A.1.3
) also holds
for
\mu_{L,U}
.

Moreover, by the definition of
\mu_{L,U}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}|\mu_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|})=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}]}\sum_{{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}]}\big|\mu_{L,U}\big(\{(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}},j\in L)\times(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}},j\in U)\}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}]}\sum_{{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{U}|\leq s-|L|\end{subarray}}\sum_{T\subseteq(L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad|\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L\setminus(((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T)}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}\big((-\infty,v^{(j)}_{p_{j}})\setminus\underline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in T}\big((v^{(j)}_{p_{j}},+\infty)\setminus\overline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\big)\times\prod_{j\in U}\{v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in(\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\setminus U}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{0<|L|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\sum_{U\subseteq\widetilde{U}}\sum_{T\subseteq(L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}]}\sum_{{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad|\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L\setminus(((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T)}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}\big((-\infty,v^{(j)}_{p_{j}})\setminus\underline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in T}\big((v^{(j)}_{p_{j}},+\infty)\setminus\overline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\big)\times\prod_{j\in U}\{v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in(\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\setminus U}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\sum_{U\subseteq\widetilde{U}}\sum_{T\subseteq(L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}]}\sum_{{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad|\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L\setminus(((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T)}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}\{v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\times\prod_{j\in U}\{v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}\}\times\prod_{j\in(\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\setminus U}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{0<|L|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\sum_{U\subseteq\widetilde{U}}\sum_{T\subseteq(L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad|\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L\setminus(((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T)}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\times\prod_{j\in U}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in(\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\setminus U}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}|\nu_{L,\widetilde{U}}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|\widetilde{U}|}).

Here, we change the order of summation in
U
and
\widetilde{U}
for
the second equality, and for the second inequality, we use the fact that

(-\infty,v^{(j)}_{p_{j}})\setminus\underline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)}\ \text{ and }\ (v^{(j)}_{p_{j}},+\infty)\setminus\overline{V}^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)}\text{ for all }j.

The last inequality follows from the observation that for each
L

and
\widetilde{U}
, the sets

\displaystyle\prod_{j\in L\setminus(((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T)}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j\in((L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U)\setminus T}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in T}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})\times\prod_{j\in U}V^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in(\widetilde{U}\setminus L)\setminus U}({\mathbb{R}}\setminus V^{(j)})

are pairwise disjoint as
U
ranges over subsets of
\widetilde{U}
and

T
ranges over subsets of
(L\cap\widetilde{U})\setminus U
.
This shows that the objective function of
(
32
) for

\mu_{L,U}
is no larger than that for
\nu_{L,U}
and
ensures that the infimum of (
32
) is
attained by some discrete signed measures

\mu_{L,U}
supported on (
33
).

Suppose
\mu_{L,U}
are discrete signed measures
supported on the lattices (
33
) that
satisfy (
A.1.3
).
We can parametrize these measures by

\mu_{L,U}\big(\{(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}},j\in L)\times(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}},j\in U)\}\big)=\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}

(34)

for
L,U\subseteq[d]
with
0<|L|+|U|\leq s
,

{\mathbf{p}}=(p_{j},j\in L)\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}]
, and

{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{j},j\in U)\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}]
.
Under this parametrization, condition
(
A.1.3
) can be written entirely in terms
of
\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\subseteq S\subseteq L\cup U\\
|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|(U\setminus L)\cap S|}\sum_{K\subseteq L\cap U}(-1)^{|(L\cap U)\setminus K|}\sum_{{\mathbf{r}}\in\prod\limits_{j\in(L\cap U)\setminus K}\underline{V}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod\limits_{j\in K}\overline{V}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod\limits_{U\setminus S}V^{(j)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\beta^{L,U}_{(m_{j},j\in(L\setminus U)\cup K;r_{j},j\in(L\cap U)\setminus K)\times(r_{j},j\in K;m_{j},j\in((L\cap U)\setminus K)\cup((U\setminus L)\cap S);r_{j},j\in U\setminus S)}=\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(f).

(35)

Consequently, (
32
) becomes

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\geq\inf\big\{\|\beta\|_{1}:\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}\text{ satisfy
\eqref{eq:equivalence-necessary-condition-discrete}}\big\}.

Since (
A.1.3
) is a system of
linear equations, there clearly exist minimizers

\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}
of the right-hand side. Let

\hat{\beta}^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}

denote one such minimizer and let
\hat{\mu}_{L,U}
be
the corresponding discrete signed measures defined via
(
34
).

Choose any constant
c_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. By construction, the function

f^{d,s}_{c_{0},\{\hat{\mu}_{L,U}\}}
is piecewise constant as
in (
28
) and satisfies
(
29
). By Lemma

5
, there exists a constant

b\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
f({\mathbf{x}})=b+f^{d,s}_{c_{0},\{\hat{\mu}_{L,U}\}}({\mathbf{x}})

for all
{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Defining
c=b+c_{0}
,
we then have
f^{d,s}_{c,\{\hat{\mu}_{L,U}\}}\equiv f
.
It follows that

\|\hat{\beta}\|_{1}=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\hat{\mu}_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\geq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\geq\|\hat{\beta}\|_{1},

so that

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\hat{\mu}_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Therefore, the minimum in the definition of
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)
is attained by discrete
signed measures. This proves
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)={V^{d,s}_{\text{XGB}}}(f)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Since the alternating-sum functional is linear, it suffices to show that
if
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST}}}
is piecewise constant as in (
28
) and
satisfies

\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(f)=0

(36)

for all
\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
and

{\mathbf{m}}\in\prod_{j\in S}[n_{j}]
, then
f
is a constant function.

Fix such a
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST}}}
.
As seen in (
30
), the expansion of

\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(f)
involves the summation over

L\subseteq S\subseteq L\cup U
with
|L|+|U|\leq s
. When
|S|>s
, this
summation is vacuous,
so that
\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(f)=0
holds automatically. Thus,
(
36
) in fact holds for all nonempty

S\subseteq[d]
.

For each
j\in[d]
, define

w^{(j)}_{0}=v^{(j)}_{1}-1\ \text{ and }\ w^{(j)}_{m_{j}}=v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\text{ for }m_{j}\in[n_{j}],

and let
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
denote the vector of evaluations of
f
at

(w^{(1)}_{m_{1}},\dots,w^{(d)}_{m_{d}})
; that is,

\phi({\mathbf{m}})=f(w^{(1)}_{m_{1}},\dots,w^{(d)}_{m_{d}})\quad\text{for }{\mathbf{m}}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}\{0,\dots,n_{j}\}.

Because
f
is piecewise constant as in (
28
)
and right-continuous, the vector
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
completely
determines
f
. Moreover, for the same reason,
(
36
) is equivalent to

\Delta^{S}_{{\mathbf{m}}}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}:=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot\phi\big(m_{j}-\delta_{j},j\in S;0,j\in S^{c}\big)=0\quad\text{for }{\mathbf{m}}=(m_{j},j\in S)\in\prod_{j\in S}[n_{j}]

(37)

for all nonempty
S
.
Thus, it suffices to show that if
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
satisfies
(
37
),
then
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
is a constant vector.

We prove this claim by induction on
d
. The case
d=1
is straightforward.
When
d=1
, (
37
) reduces to

\Delta^{\{1\}}_{m}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}=\phi(m)-\phi(m-1)=0\quad\text{for }m\in[n_{1}].

Hence, in this case, it is clear that
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
is a constant vector.
Suppose the claim holds for
d-1
, and let us prove it for
d
.
For each
m_{d}\in\{0,\dots,n_{d}\}
, let
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d})}
denote the
subvector of
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
with last index
m_{d}
; that is,

\phi^{(m_{d})}(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1})=\phi(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1},m_{d})\quad\text{for }(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1})\in\prod_{j=1}^{d-1}\{0,\dots,n_{j}\}.

Clearly,
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(0)}
satisfies
(
37
) for
d-1
. Note that
for
m_{d}\in[n_{d}]
,

\Delta^{S}_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1})}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d})}-\Delta^{S}_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1})}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d}-1)}=\Delta^{S\cup\{d\}}_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1},m_{d})}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}=0

for every nonempty
S\subseteq[d-1]
and
(m_{1},\dots,m_{d-1})\in\prod_{j=1}^{d-1}[n_{j}]
. Thus, all
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d})}
satisfy
(
37
) for
d-1
.
By the induction hypothesis, it follows that each
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d})}
is a
constant vector. Lastly, taking
S=\{d\}
in
(
37
) gives

\Delta^{\{d\}}_{m_{d}}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}=\phi(0,\dots,0,m_{d})-\phi(0,\dots,0,m_{d}-1)=0\quad\text{for }m_{d}\in[n_{d}].

Thus, the constants in
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}^{(m_{d})}
are the same for all
m_{d}
,
which means that
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}
is a constant vector.
∎

A.1.4
Proof of (
13
)

We use the following standard result from real analysis in the proof.

Theorem 6

(Theorem 3.29 of
13
)
.

Suppose
f:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
has finite total variation and is
right-continuous. Then, there exists a unique constant
c\in{\mathbb{R}}

and a unique finite signed Borel measure
\lambda
on
{\mathbb{R}}
such that

f(x)=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq t)\,d\lambda(t)\quad\text{ for }x\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(38)

Conversely, if
f:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is of the form
(
38
), then
f
has finite total
variation, is right-continuous, and

{\text{TV}}(f)=\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}
.

Proof of (
13
).

We first show that

\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}=\big\{f:{\text{TV}}(f)<\infty\text{ and }f\text{ is right-continuous}\big\}

and that

V^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)={\text{TV}}(f)

for
f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
.

Suppose

f=f^{1,1}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}\in\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
.
Since
d=s=1
, the only admissible pairs of
(L,U)
with

0<|L|+|U|\leq s
are
(\{1\},\emptyset)
and

(\emptyset,\{1\})
.
Thus,
f
can be expressed as

f(x)=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\nu_{\{1\},\emptyset}(l)+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x<u)\,d\nu_{\emptyset,\{1\}}(u)

for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Define a signed Borel measure
\lambda
by

\lambda=\nu_{\{1\},\emptyset}-\nu_{\emptyset,\{1\}}
.
Then,

f(x)=c+\nu_{\emptyset,\{1\}}({\mathbb{R}})+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda(l)

for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and

\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}=|\lambda|({\mathbb{R}})\leq|\nu_{\{1\},\emptyset}|({\mathbb{R}})+|\nu_{\emptyset,\{1\}}|({\mathbb{R}})=\|\nu_{\{1\},\emptyset}\|_{\text{TV}}+\|\nu_{\emptyset,\{1\}}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Hence, every
f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
admits
the simpler representation

f_{c,\lambda}(x):=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda(l),

and its complexity
V^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)
can be computed by

V^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)=\inf\{\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}:f_{c,\lambda}\equiv f\}.

By Theorem
6
, the collection of such
functions
f_{c,\lambda}
is precisely the collection of all
right-continuous functions with finite total variation. Moreover,
the pair
(c,\lambda)
with
f_{c,\lambda}\equiv f
is unique and
satisfies
\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}={\text{TV}}(f)
. Consequently,

\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}=\big\{f:{\text{TV}}(f)<\infty\text{ and }f\text{ is right-continuous}\big\},

and for every
f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
,

V^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)={\text{TV}}(f).

We next prove that

V^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big({\text{TV}}(f)+|\Delta(f)|\big)

for all
f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{ST}}(=\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}})
.
By the same argument as above, we can show that every

f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
admits the representation

f_{c,\lambda,\mu}(x):=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda(l)+\int{\mathbf{1}}(l\leq x<u)\,d\mu(l,u),

where
\lambda
and
\mu
are finite signed Borel measures on
{\mathbb{R}}
and

{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, respectively, and its complexity
V^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)

is given by

V^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)=\inf\big\{\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}+\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}:f_{c,\lambda,\mu}\equiv f\big\}.

First, suppose

f\equiv f_{c,\lambda,\mu}\in\mathcal{F}^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{ST}}
.
For every
x<y
, we have

\displaystyle|f(x)-f(y)|

\displaystyle=\big|-\lambda((x,y])+\mu\big((-\infty,x]\times(x,y]\big)-\mu\big((x,y]\times(y,+\infty)\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda|((x,y])+|\mu|\big({\mathbb{R}}\times(x,y]\big)+|\mu|\big((x,y]\times{\mathbb{R}}\big),

and it thus follows that

{\text{TV}}(f)\leq\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}+2\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}.

Moreover, we have

|\Delta(f)|=\big|\lim_{x\to+\infty}f(x)-\lim_{x\to-\infty}f(x)\big|=|\lambda({\mathbb{R}})|\leq\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}.

Combining these two inequalities, we obtain

\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big({\text{TV}}(f)+|\Delta(f)|\big)\leq\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}+\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}.

Taking the infimum over all
\lambda
and
\mu
with

f_{c,\lambda,\mu}\equiv f
gives

\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big({\text{TV}}(f)+|\Delta(f)|\big)\leq V^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f),

which proves one direction of the desired identity.

We now prove the reverse inequality.
Suppose
f\in\mathcal{F}^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{ST}}(=\mathcal{F}^{1,1}_{\infty-\text{ST}})
. Since
f
has finite total
variation and is right-continuous,
Theorem
6
guarantees the existence of a
constant
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
and a finite signed Borel measure
\lambda
on

{\mathbb{R}}
such that

f(x)=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda(l)\quad\text{for }x\in{\mathbb{R}}

and
\|\lambda\|_{\text{TV}}={\text{TV}}(f)
.
Let
\lambda=\lambda^{+}-\lambda^{-}
be the Jordan decomposition of

\lambda
, and let
(P,N)
be a Hahn decomposition.
Without loss of generality, we assume

\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})\geq\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}}).

Then,

|\Delta(f)|=\big|\lim_{x\to+\infty}f(x)-\lim_{x\to-\infty}f(x)\big|=|\lambda({\mathbb{R}})|=\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})-\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}}),

and thus,

\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big({\text{TV}}(f)+|\Delta(f)|\big)=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Big(\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})+\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})+\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})-\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})\Big)=\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}}).

Define a Borel measure
\widetilde{\lambda}
on
{\mathbb{R}}
by

\widetilde{\lambda}(E)=\Big(1-\frac{\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\Big)\cdot\lambda^{+}(E)

for Borel sets
E\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
. The assumption

\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})\geq\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})
ensures that
\widetilde{\lambda}(E)

is nonnegative for all
E
.
Next, define a signed Borel measure
\mu
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
by

\mu(E_{1}\times E_{2})=\frac{1}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\cdot\Big(\lambda^{+}(E_{1})\cdot\lambda^{-}(E_{2})-\lambda^{-}(E_{1})\cdot\lambda^{+}(E_{2})\Big)

for Borel sets
E_{1},E_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
.
By construction,

\mu(E_{1}\times E_{2})=\begin{cases}\lambda^{+}(E_{1})\cdot\lambda^{-}(E_{2})/\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})\geq 0&\text{if }E_{1}\subseteq P,E_{2}\subseteq N,\\
-\lambda^{-}(E_{1})\cdot\lambda^{+}(E_{2})/\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})\leq 0&\text{if }E_{1}\subseteq N,E_{2}\subseteq P,\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

and therefore,

\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}=\mu(P\times N)-\mu(N\times P)=\frac{2\lambda^{+}(P)\cdot\lambda^{-}(N)}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}=2\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}}).

Define a signed Borel measure
\widetilde{\mu}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
by

d\widetilde{\mu}(l,u)=1(l\leq u)\cdot d\mu(l,u).

Since
\mu
is anti-symmetric, i.e.,

\mu(E_{1}\times E_{2})=-\mu(E_{2}\times E_{1})
for all Borel sets

E_{1},E_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

\|\widetilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}=\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}}).

Hence,

\|\widetilde{\lambda}\|_{\text{TV}}+\|\widetilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\widetilde{\lambda}({\mathbb{R}})+\|\widetilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})-\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})+\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})=\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}}).

Now, observe that

\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\widetilde{\lambda}(l)=\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda^{+}(l)-\frac{\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\cdot\lambda^{+}((-\infty,x])

and that

\displaystyle\int{\mathbf{1}}(l\leq x<u)\,d\widetilde{\mu}(l,u)=\int{\mathbf{1}}(l\leq x<u)\,d\mu(l,u)=\mu\big((-\infty,x]\times(x,+\infty)\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\cdot\Big(\lambda^{+}((-\infty,x])\cdot\lambda^{-}((x,+\infty))-\lambda^{-}((-\infty,x])\cdot\lambda^{+}((x,+\infty))\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\cdot\lambda^{+}((-\infty,x])-\lambda^{-}((-\infty,x])=\frac{\lambda^{-}({\mathbb{R}})}{\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})}\cdot\lambda^{+}((-\infty,x])-\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda^{-}(l)

for every
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Combining these two equations gives

f_{c,\widetilde{\lambda},\widetilde{\mu}}(x)=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\widetilde{\lambda}(l)+\int{\mathbf{1}}(l\leq x<u)\,d\widetilde{\mu}(l,u)=c+\int{\mathbf{1}}(x\geq l)\,d\lambda(l)=f(x)

for every
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
. As a result,

V^{1,2}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}(f)\leq\|\widetilde{\lambda}\|_{\text{TV}}+\|\widetilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\lambda^{+}({\mathbb{R}})=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\big({\text{TV}}(f)+|\Delta(f)|\big),

which proves the reverse inequality.
∎

A.1.5
Proof of Proposition
5

In the proof of Proposition
5
, we use the
following theorem, which connects functions on a compact domain with
finite Hardy–Krause variation to the cumulative distribution functions
of finite signed Borel measures on the same domain. This result will
also play a central role in the proofs of
Propositions
3
and
7
.

To state the result, we first
recall the definition of Hardy–Krause variation on compact domains.
Let
f:\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]\to{\mathbb{R}}
and

{\mathbf{a}}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{m})\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}\{u_{j},v_{j}\}
.
For each
S\subseteq[m]
, define

f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)=f(x_{j},j\in S;a_{j},j\in S^{c})\quad\text{for }(x_{j},j\in S)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}.

Since the domain is compact, there is no need to take limits as
in (
9
).
The Hardy–Krause variation of
f
anchored at
{\mathbf{a}}
on

\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]
is then defined by

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Big(f;\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]\Big)=\sum_{0<|S|\leq m}{\text{Vit}}\Big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})};\prod_{j\in S}[u_{j},v_{j}]\Big).

Theorem 7

(Theorem 3 of
1
)
.

Suppose
f:\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]\to{\mathbb{R}}
is
right-continuous and has finite Hardy–Krause variation anchored at

{\mathbf{a}}=(u_{1},\dots,u_{m})
. Then, there exists a unique finite
signed Borel measure
\nu
on
\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]
such that

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\nu\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},x_{j}]\Big)\quad\text{for }(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}].

(39)

Conversely, if
f
is of the form (
39
), then
f
has
finite Hardy–Krause variation anchored at
{\mathbf{a}}
and

\|\nu\|_{\text{TV}}={\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}\Big(f;\prod_{j=1}^{m}[u_{j},v_{j}]\Big)+|f({\mathbf{a}})|.

Proof of Proposition
5
.

To prove (
15
), it suffices to show that

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)/\min(2^{s}-1,2^{d})\leq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\leq{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)

for each anchor point
{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
.
Here, we prove this inequality only for the case

{\mathbf{a}}=(-\infty,\dots,-\infty)
. The same argument applies to
the other anchor point choices.

Recall that
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
is the collection of all functions

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
of the form (
7
).
For each
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
, by modifying
each basis function
b^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}}
as

\displaystyle b^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})

(40)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{j\in L\setminus U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U\setminus L}\big(1-{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq u_{j})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in L\cap U}\big({\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})-{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq u_{j})\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in L\cap U}{\mathbf{1}}(l_{j}\leq u_{j}),

we can represent
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
as

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d}):=b+\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(t_{j},j\in S)

(41)

for some
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and finite signed Borel measures
\mu_{S}

on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
, where the summation runs over all nonempty

S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
.
Specifically, each
\mu_{S}
is related to
the original measures
\nu_{L,U}
by

\displaystyle\mu_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S}E_{j}\Big)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\subseteq S\subseteq L\cup U\\
|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|(U\setminus L)\cap S|}\sum_{K\subseteq L\cap U}(-1)^{|(L\cap U)\setminus K|}

(42)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}\Big(\prod_{j\in(L\setminus U)\cup K}E_{j}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{|(L\cap U)\setminus K|}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{|(U\setminus S)\cup K|}\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in((L\cap U)\setminus K)\\
\hskip 8.19447pt\cup((U\setminus L)\cap S)\end{subarray}}E_{j}\Big)

for Borel sets
E_{j}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}
for
j\in S
,
where
\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}
are the signed Borel measures on

{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}
defined by

d\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})=\prod_{j\in L\cap U}{\mathbf{1}}(l_{j}\leq u_{j})\cdot d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}).

This relationship between
\mu_{S}
and
\nu_{L,U}
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}|\mu_{S}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|S|})\leq\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\subseteq S\subseteq L\cup U\\
|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{K\subseteq L\cap U}|\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|})

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{S:L\subseteq S\subseteq L\cup U,S\neq\emptyset}\sum_{K\subseteq L\cap U}|\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|})

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\big({\mathbf{1}}(L\neq\emptyset)\cdot 2^{|U\setminus L|}+{\mathbf{1}}(L=\emptyset)\cdot(2^{|U\setminus L|}-1)\big)\cdot 2^{|L\cap U|}\cdot|\widebar{\nu}_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\min(2^{s}-1,2^{d})\cdot\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}|\nu_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}).

(43)

Define

V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)=\inf\Big\{\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}:f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}\equiv f\Big\}\quad\text{for }f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}.

(44)

By (
A.1.5
), we have

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\leq V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)\leq\min(2^{s}-1,2^{d})\cdot{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\quad\text{for every }f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}.

Thus, it suffices to prove that

V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)={\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)\quad\text{for every }f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}.

Fix
f\equiv f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
.
For each nonempty
S\subseteq[d]
, we have

f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)=b+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R:\emptyset\neq R\subseteq S\\
|R|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|R|}}\prod_{j\in R}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{R}(t_{j},j\in R).

Hence, for each nonempty
T\subseteq[d]
,

\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R:\emptyset\neq R\subseteq T\\
|R|\leq s\end{subarray}}\Big(\sum_{S:R\subseteq S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\Big)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|R|}}\prod_{j\in R}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{R}(t_{j},j\in R).

The inner sum vanishes unless
R=T
, in which case it equals
1
.
Therefore, if
|T|\leq s
,

\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|T|}}\prod_{j\in T}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{T}(t_{j},j\in T),

while if
|T|>s
, the expression vanishes.

Now, fix a nonempty
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
.
Since

{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})={\text{Vit}}\Big((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)\Big),

we have

\displaystyle{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})

\displaystyle={\text{Vit}}\Big((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|T|}}\prod_{j\in T}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{T}(t_{j},j\in T)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sup_{u_{j}<v_{j},j\in T}{\text{Vit}}\Big((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|T|}}\prod_{j\in T}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{T}(t_{j},j\in T);\prod_{j\in T}[u_{j},v_{j}]\Big)

\displaystyle=\sup_{u_{j}<v_{j},j\in T}{\text{Vit}}\bigg((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\mu_{T}\Big(\prod_{j\in T}(u_{j},x_{j}]\Big);\prod_{j\in T}[u_{j},v_{j}]\bigg).

Moreover, by Theorem
7
,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\text{Vit}}\bigg((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\mu_{T}\Big(\prod_{j\in T}(u_{j},x_{j}]\Big);\prod_{j\in T}[u_{j},v_{j}]\bigg)

\displaystyle={\text{HK}}_{(u_{j},j\in T)}\bigg((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\mu_{T}\Big(\prod_{j\in T}(u_{j},x_{j}]\Big);\prod_{j\in T}[u_{j},v_{j}]\bigg)=|\mu_{T}|\Big(\prod_{j\in T}(u_{j},v_{j}]\Big).

Here, the first equality holds because the map vanishes on every section
containing the anchor point

(u_{j},j\in T)
; that is,
it becomes zero whenever
x_{j}=u_{j}
for some
j\in T
.
Consequently,

{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})=|\mu_{T}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|T|})=\|\mu_{T}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Since

{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})=0

for all
|T|>s
, it follows that

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)=\sum_{0<|T|\leq d}{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})=\sum_{0<|T|\leq s}{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})=\sum_{0<|T|\leq s}\|\mu_{T}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Thus, there is in fact no need to take the infimum in
(
44
), and

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)=V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f),

which completes the proof of (
15
).

We now investigate the tightness of the inequalities in
(
15
). Fix
{\mathbf{a}}=(-\infty,\dots,-\infty)
.
First, observe that for the function

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})={\mathbf{1}}(x_{1}\geq 0)
, we have

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)=1={\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f).

This shows that the right inequality in (
15
)
is tight.

To show that the left inequality in (
15
) is
also tight, we consider two cases, depending on whether
s\leq d

or
s>d
. In the case
s\leq d
, consider the function

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})={\mathbf{1}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{s}<0)
. It is clear that

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)=1
. Moreover, since

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\prod_{j=1}^{s}\big(1-{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq 0)\big)=1+\sum_{l=1}^{s}(-1)^{l}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\cdots<j_{l}\leq s}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j_{1}}\geq 0,\dots,x_{j_{l}}\geq 0),

it follows that

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)=2^{s}-1.

This shows that the left inequality in (
15
)
is tight when
s\leq d
. In the case
s>d
, consider the function

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})={\mathbf{1}}(-1\leq x_{1},\dots,x_{s-d}<0,\ x_{s-d+1},\dots,x_{d}<0)
. Again, it is clear that

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)=1
. Furthermore, we can write

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\prod_{j=1}^{s-d}\big({\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq-1)-{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq 0)\big)\cdot\prod_{j=s-d+1}^{d}\big(1-{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq 0)\big),

from which we obtain

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)=2^{d}.

This shows that the left inequality in (
15
)
is tight when
s>d
.
∎

A.1.6
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof of Proposition
2
.

First, (a) follows from the right-continuity of each basis function

b^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}}
and the dominated
convergence theorem, together with the fact that each signed measure

\nu_{L,U}
is finite.

For (b) and (c), recall the alternative representation

f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}
of
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
, given in
(
41
) and
introduced in the proof of Proposition
5
.
Since the sum in this representation ranges over all

S\subseteq[d]
with
0<|S|\leq s
, the function
class
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
enlarges as
s
increases. This yields (b). Since

|S|
is always bounded by
d
, the class
{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
remains unchanged
once
s\geq d
, which proves (c).

Lastly, (d) follows immediately from the definition.
∎

A.1.7
Proof of Proposition
3

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Step 1:
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,d}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}

\Rightarrow

f
is right-continuous,
and
{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)<\infty
for all
{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
.

This follows directly from Propositions
2

and
5
.

Step 2:
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}

\Rightarrow

(
11
) holds for all

S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|>s
.

We only consider the case
{\mathbf{a}}=(-\infty,\cdots,-\infty)
;
the argument for other choices of anchor points is entirely analogous.
Suppose that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
. Recall from the proof of
Proposition
5
that
f
admits the
alternative representation
f\equiv f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}
of the
form (
41
) for some

b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and finite signed Borel measures
\mu_{S}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
.

Fix
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|>s
. Using this
representation, we can express
f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})}
as

f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})}(x_{j},j\in T)=b+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S:\emptyset\neq S\subseteq T\\
|S|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(t_{j},j\in S).

It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|T|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in T}\delta_{j}}\cdot f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})}\big((1-\delta_{j})w_{j}+\delta_{j}v_{j},j\in T\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S:\emptyset\neq S\subseteq T\\
|S|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{S}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}\bigg(\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{T\setminus S}\in\{0,1\}^{|T\setminus S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in T\setminus S}\delta_{j}}\bigg)\cdot(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(t_{j},j\in S),

where
{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{S}=(\delta_{j},j\in S)
and

{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{T\setminus S}=(\delta_{j},j\in T\setminus S)
.
In the last expression, since
|S|\leq s<|T|
, the innermost
sum always vanishes. This proves that
(
11
) holds for
T
.

Step 3:
f
is right-continuous, and

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)<\infty
for some
{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}

\Rightarrow

f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,d}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
.

Assume that
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is right-continuous and that

{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f)<\infty
for some
{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
.
Fix a nonempty
S\subseteq[d]
, and for each integer
N\geq 1
,
define
g^{S}_{N}:{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

g^{S}_{N}(x_{j},j\in S)=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big((1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}(-N),j\in S\big).

Clearly,
g^{S}_{N}
inherits the right-continuity of
f
on the
coordinates
j\in S
. Moreover,

{\text{HK}}_{(-N,j\in S)}\big(g^{S}_{N};[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)={\text{Vit}}\big(g^{S}_{N};[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)={\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})};[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)<\infty.

Here, the first equality follows from the fact that
g^{S}_{N}
vanishes
whenever
x_{j}=-N
for some
j\in S
. Hence, by
Theorem
7
, there exists a unique finite signed
Borel measure
\nu^{S}_{N}
on
[-N,N]^{|S|}
such that

g^{S}_{N}(x_{j},j\in S)=\nu^{S}_{N}\Big(\prod_{j\in S}(-N,x_{j}]\Big)\quad\text{for }(x_{j},j\in S)\in[-N,N]^{|S|}.

Here, the endpoint
-N
could be excluded from the intervals because

g^{S}_{N}
becomes zero if
x_{j}=-N
for some
j\in S
.
Furthermore, Theorem
7
also gives

|\nu^{S}_{N}|([-N,N]^{|S|})={\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})};[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)\leq{\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big)<\infty.

Now, fix integers
N_{2}>N_{1}\geq 1
, and observe that

g^{S}_{N_{1}}(x_{j},j\in S)=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot g^{S}_{N_{2}}\big((1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}(-N_{1}),j\in S\big)=\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}\Big(\prod_{j\in S}(-N_{1},x_{j}]\Big)

for every
(x_{j},j\in S)\in[-N_{1},N_{1}]^{|S|}
.
By uniqueness of
\nu^{S}_{N_{1}}
, this means that the restriction of

\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}
to
[-N_{1},N_{1}]^{|S|}
coincides with
\nu^{S}_{N_{1}}
.
Hence,
\{\nu^{S}_{N}\}_{N\geq 1}
forms a sequence of finite signed Borel
measures, where
\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}
is an extension of
\nu^{S}_{N_{1}}
whenever

N_{2}>N_{1}
.

Using this sequence of signed measures, we define a finite signed Borel
measure
\nu_{S}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
extending
\nu^{S}_{N}
for all
N\geq 1
.
Specifically, we define
\nu_{S}
by

\nu_{S}(E)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu^{S}_{N}(E\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})\quad\text{for Borel sets }E\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}.

We first verify that
\nu_{S}(E)
is well-defined for each Borel set
E
.
For integers
M>N\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\big|\nu^{S}_{M}(E\cap[-M,M]^{|S|})-\nu^{S}_{N}(E\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\nu^{S}_{M}\big(E\cap([-M,M]^{|S|}\setminus[-N,N]^{|S|})\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq|\nu^{S}_{M}|\big([-M,M]^{|S|}\setminus[-N,N]^{|S|}\big).

Since

\sup_{N\geq 1}|\nu^{S}_{N}|([-N,N]^{|S|})\leq{\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big)<\infty,

for every
\epsilon>0
, there exists an integer
N_{0}\geq 1
such that

|\nu^{S}_{M}|\big([-M,M]^{|S|}\setminus[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)<\epsilon\quad\text{for all }M>N\geq N_{0}.

Thus,
\{\nu^{S}_{N}(E\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})\}_{N\geq 1}

forms a Cauchy sequence, and hence,
\nu_{S}(E)
is well-defined.

Next, we show that
\nu_{S}
is countably additive. Suppose

E=\cup_{k\geq 1}E_{k}
for disjoint Borel sets
E_{k}
.
For integers
N_{2}>N_{1}\geq 1
, since
\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}
extends
\nu^{S}_{N_{1}}
,
the restriction of
|\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}|
(the variation of
\nu^{S}_{N_{2}}
) to

[-N_{1},N_{1}]^{|S|}
also coincides with
|\nu^{S}_{N_{1}}|
. Thus, for each

k\geq 1
,

\big\{|\nu^{S}_{N}|(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})\big\}_{N\geq 1}

is an increasing sequence of nonnegative numbers. By the monotone
convergence theorem, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}\lim_{N\to\infty}|\nu^{S}_{N}|(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{k\geq 1}|\nu^{S}_{N}|(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}|\nu^{S}_{N}|(E\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})\leq\sup_{N\geq 1}|\nu^{S}_{N}|([-N,N]^{|S|})\leq{\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big)<\infty.

Moreover, since

\big|\nu^{S}_{N}\big(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)\big|\leq|\nu^{S}_{N}|\big(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)\leq\lim_{N\to\infty}|\nu^{S}_{N}|\big(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|}\big)

for each
k
and
N
, the dominated convergence theorem yields

\displaystyle\nu_{S}(E)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu^{S}_{N}(E\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{k\geq 1}\nu^{S}_{N}(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\lim_{N\to\infty}\nu^{S}_{N}(E_{k}\cap[-N,N]^{|S|})=\sum_{k\geq 1}\nu_{S}(E_{k}).

This establishes that
\nu_{S}
is countably additive.

For each
N\geq 1
, it is clear from the definition of
\nu_{S}
that for any
Borel set
E\subseteq[-N,N]^{|S|}
, we have
\nu_{S}(E)=\nu^{S}_{N}(E)
.
Furthermore,

|\nu_{S}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|S|})=\lim_{N\to\infty}|\nu_{S}|([-N,N]^{|S|})=\lim_{N\to\infty}|\nu^{S}_{N}|([-N,N]^{|S|})\leq{\text{Vit}}\big(f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big)<\infty.

Hence,
\nu_{S}
is a finite signed Borel measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}

extending
\nu^{S}_{N}
for all
N\geq 1
, as desired.

Define
N_{{\mathbf{a}}}=\{j\in[d]:a_{j}=-\infty\}
. For each integer

N\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big((1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}(-N),j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}};(1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}N,j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot g^{S}_{N}\big((1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}(-N),j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}};(1-\delta_{j})x_{j}+\delta_{j}N,j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}\big)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}|}\cdot\nu_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(-N,x_{j}]\times\prod_{j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(x_{j},N]\Big).

Taking the limit as
N\to\infty
yields

\sum_{R\subseteq S}(-1)^{|S|-|R|}\cdot f^{R}_{(a_{j},j\in R^{c})}(x_{j},j\in R)=\widetilde{\nu}_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(-\infty,x_{j}]\times\prod_{j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(x_{j},+\infty)\Big)

where
\widetilde{\nu}_{S}
is the signed Borel measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
defined
by

\widetilde{\nu}_{S}=(-1)^{|S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}|}\cdot\nu_{S}
.
Moreover, since

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\lim_{{\mathbf{z}}\to{\mathbf{a}}}f({\mathbf{z}})+\sum_{S:\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]}\sum_{R\subseteq S}(-1)^{|S|-|R|}\cdot f^{R}_{(a_{j},j\in R^{c})}(x_{j},j\in R)\quad\text{for }(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

it follows that

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\mathbf{z}}\to{\mathbf{a}}}f({\mathbf{z}})+\sum_{S:\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]}\widetilde{\nu}_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(-\infty,x_{j}]\times\prod_{j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}(x_{j},+\infty)\Big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\mathbf{z}}\to{\mathbf{a}}}f({\mathbf{z}})+\sum_{S:\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[d]}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}}\Big(\prod_{j\in S\cap N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\Big)\cdot\Big(\prod_{j\in S\setminus N_{{\mathbf{a}}}}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<t_{j})\Big)\,d\widetilde{\nu}_{S}(t_{j},j\in S)

for all
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
This proves that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,d}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
.

Step 4:
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,d}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
and (
11
)
holds for all
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|>s

\Rightarrow

f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
.

Now, we assume that
f
additionally satisfies condition
(
11
) for all
S\subseteq[d]
with

|S|>s
. Since the additional condition is vacuous when
s\geq d
, we
assume that
s<d
. Here, we present the argument only for the case

{\mathbf{a}}=(-\infty,\cdots,-\infty)
, but the proof for other anchor
points is entirely analogous.

Since
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,d}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
,
f
admits the alternative representation
(
41
) for some
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
finite signed Borel measures
\mu_{S}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
.
For each
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|>s
, we have

\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\delta}}\in\{0,1\}^{|S|}}(-1)^{\sum_{j\in S}\delta_{j}}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}\big((1-\delta_{j})v_{j}+\delta_{j}u_{j},j\in S\big)=\mu_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S}(u_{j},v_{j}]\Big)

for all
u_{j}<v_{j},j\in S
. Therefore, condition
(
11
) implies that for all such
S

and all
u_{j}<v_{j},j\in S
,

\mu_{S}\Big(\prod_{j\in S}(u_{j},v_{j}]\Big)=0.

By Dynkin’s
\pi
-
\lambda
theorem, this forces
\mu_{S}=0
.
Hence, all integrals over
\mu_{S}
with
|S|>s
can be
dropped from (
41
), and we can
conclude that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
.
∎

A.1.8
Proof of Proposition
6

Proof of Proposition
6
.

Fix
j_{0}\in[d]
and
t_{j_{0}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Define
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}

as in the statement of the proposition with
j=j_{0}
.
By symmetry, it suffices to show that
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(g)\leq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)
.

Suppose that
f\equiv{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
. For each
L,U\subseteq[d]
with

0<|L|+|U|\leq s
, we define a signed Borel measure
\mu_{L,U}

on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}
as follows. If
j_{0}\notin L\cup U
,
set
\mu_{L,U}=\nu_{L,U}
. If
j_{0}\in L\setminus U
, define

\mu_{L,U}
as the pushforward of

\nu_{L\setminus\{j_{0}\},U\cup\{j_{0}\}}
under the map

\big((l_{j},j\in L\setminus\{j_{0}\}),(u_{j},j\in U\cup\{j_{0}\})\big)\mapsto\big((l_{j},j\in L\setminus\{j_{0}\};t_{j_{0}}-u_{j_{0}}),(u_{j},j\in U)\big),

if
j_{0}\in U\setminus L
, define
\mu_{L,U}
as the
pushforward of
\nu_{L\cup\{j_{0}\},U\setminus\{j_{0}\}}
under
the map

\big((l_{j},j\in L\cup\{j_{0}\}),(u_{j},j\in U\setminus\{j_{0}\})\big)\mapsto\big((l_{j},j\in L),(u_{j},j\in U\setminus\{j_{0}\};t_{j_{0}}-l_{j_{0}})\big),

and if
j_{0}\in L\cap U
, define
\mu_{L,U}
as the pushforward
of
\nu_{L,U}
under the map

\big((l_{j},j\in L),(u_{j},j\in U)\big)\mapsto\big((l_{j},j\in L\setminus\{j_{0}\};t_{j_{0}}-u_{j_{0}}),(u_{j},j\in U\setminus\{j_{0}\};t_{j_{0}}-l_{j_{0}})\big).

With these definitions, one readily checks that

f^{d,s}_{c,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}\equiv g
.
Moreover, there is a one-to-one correspondence between
the signed measures
\mu_{L,U}
and the signed measures

\nu_{L,U}
, under which the corresponding signed measures have
the same total variation.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(g)\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\not\in L\cup U\end{subarray}}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in L\setminus U\end{subarray}}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in U\setminus L\end{subarray}}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in L\cap U\end{subarray}}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\not\in L\cup U\end{subarray}}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in L\setminus U\end{subarray}}\|\nu_{L\setminus\{j_{0}\},U\cup\{j_{0}\}}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in U\setminus L\end{subarray}}\|\nu_{L\cup\{j_{0}\},U\setminus\{j_{0}\}}\|_{\text{TV}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0<|L|+|U|\leq s\\
j_{0}\in L\cap U\end{subarray}}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Taking the infimum over all representations
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
of
f

yields
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(g)\leq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)
.
∎

A.2
Proofs of Theorem and Lemma in Section
4

A.2.1
Proof of Theorem
3

Since the latter part of the theorem is a direct consequence of
Lemma
1
, we only prove the former part
concerning existence here. The proof of
Theorem
2
is entirely analogous.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

When the signed measures
\nu_{L,U}
satisfy condition (a) of
Lemma
1
, the corresponding function

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
can be written as

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=c+\sum_{(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J}\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x_{j}\geq(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x_{j}<(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2\big)

where

J=\bigg\{(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}):L,U\subseteq[d],0<|L|+|U|\leq s,{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}-1],\text{ and }{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}-1]\bigg\}

(45)

and

\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}=\nu_{L,U}\Big(\big\{\big((v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2,j\in L;(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2,j\in U\big)\big\}\Big)

for each
(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J
, with

{\mathbf{p}}=(p_{j},j\in L)
and
{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{j},j\in U)
.

Let
(\hat{c},(\hat{\beta}^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}},(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J))
be a solution to the
finite-dimensional optimization problem

\displaystyle\text{argmin}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg(y_{i}-c-\sum_{(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J}\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x^{(i)}_{j}\geq(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x^{(i)}_{j}<(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2\big)\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\text{s.t.}\sum_{(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J}|\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}|\leq V.

The existence of such a solution is immediate.
Define
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\hat{c}+\sum_{(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J}\hat{\beta}^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}\cdot\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x_{j}\geq(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x_{j}<(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2\big).

By construction,
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST},\bullet}}
, and it is a solution to
the problem (
16
). Moreover,
Lemma
1
implies that it is also a solution to
(
3
).
∎

A.2.2
Proof of Lemma
1

We use the following lemma for the proof.
This lemma is proved right after the proof of
Lemma
1
.

Lemma 6
.

For every
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
, there exists

f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST}}}
with discrete signed
measures
\mu_{L,U}
supported on the lattices (
17
)
such that

(a)

f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})={f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})
for
i=1,\dots,n

(b)

\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

For
z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, define

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\inf\bigg\{\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}:{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})=z_{i}\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n\bigg\}.

For simplicity, we suppress the dependence on the design points

{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
.
By definition,

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}))\leq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\quad\text{for every }f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}.

By Lemma
6
, for each

z_{1},\dots,z_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

\displaystyle=\inf\bigg\{\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}:{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})=z_{i}\text{ for }i=1,\dots,n,

\displaystyle\text{ with }\nu_{L,U}\text{ supported on the lattices \eqref{lattice}}\bigg\}.

Recall the index set
J
from (
45
), and let
{\mathbf{x}}

denote the
n\times|J|
matrix with entries

X_{i,(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})}=\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x^{(i)}_{j}\geq(v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}\big(x^{(i)}_{j}<(v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2\big)

for
i=1,\dots,n
and
(L,U,{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})\in J
.
We parametrize discrete signed measures
\nu_{L,U}

supported on the lattices (
17
) by

\nu_{L,U}\big(\{((v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2,j\in L)\times((v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2,j\in U)\}\big)=\beta^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}}

(46)

for
L,U\subseteq[d]
with
0<|L|+|U|\leq s
,

{\mathbf{p}}=(p_{j},j\in L)\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}-1]
,
and
{\mathbf{q}}=(q_{j},j\in U)\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}-1]
.
With this parametrization, we can express
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
as

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\inf\big\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}:{\mathbf{x}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}={\mathbf{z}}-c{\mathbf{1}}\text{ for some }c\in{\mathbb{R}}\big\},

(47)

where
{\mathbf{z}}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
, and
{\mathbf{1}}
is the all-ones
vector.

Next, we show that if the set

\mathcal{D}_{{\mathbf{z}}}:=\big\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|J|}:{\mathbf{x}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}={\mathbf{z}}-c{\mathbf{1}}\text{ for some }c\in{\mathbb{R}}\big\}

(48)

is nonempty, which is clearly the case when

{\mathbf{z}}=(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}))

for some
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
, then there exists
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
that achieves
the minimum in (
47
).
Fix
{\mathbf{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that
\mathcal{D}_{{\mathbf{z}}}
is
nonempty, and suppose
{\mathbf{x}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}={\mathbf{z}}-c_{0}{\mathbf{1}}

for some
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}
and
c_{0}
.
Clearly, the infimum on the right-hand side of
(
47
) remains unchanged if we further
constrain
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
to satisfy
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{1}
:

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\inf\big\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}:{\mathbf{x}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}={\mathbf{z}}-c{\mathbf{1}}\text{ for some }c\in{\mathbb{R}}\text{ and }\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{1}\big\}.

It is also straightforward to verify that the set

\mathcal{D}_{{\mathbf{z}}}\cap\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|J|}:\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}\|_{1}\}

is nonempty, closed, and bounded.
Since the map
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\mapsto\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{1}
is continuous, it follows
that there exists
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
that attains the minimum in
(
47
).

Using the results established above, we now prove the lemma.
Fix
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
, and let
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
be the minimizer of
(
47
) for
{\mathbf{z}}=(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}))
.
Let
\hat{c}
be the corresponding constant from
(
48
), and let

\nu_{L,U}
denote the signed Borel measures associated with

\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
via (
46
).
By construction,
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST}}}

satisfies the first two conditions of the lemma.
Moreover, since

\displaystyle{\displaystyle V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}))=\|\hat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\|_{1}=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\geq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}({f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}})\geq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)})),

we have

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}({f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}})=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}={V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)}),\dots,f({\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}))\leq{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f).

Hence,
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST},\bullet}}
is a function that satisfies
all the desired properties.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6
.

For each
j\in[d]
, define

I^{(j)}_{m_{j}}=(v^{(j)}_{m_{j}},v^{(j)}_{m_{j}+1}]\qquad\text{for }m_{j}\in[n_{j}-1],

and

\underline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}=(v^{(j)}_{1},v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}]\ \text{ and }\ \overline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}=(v^{(j)}_{m_{j}},v^{(j)}_{n_{j}}]\qquad\text{for }m_{j}\in[n_{j}].

Also, let

\underline{O}^{(j)}=(-\infty,v^{(j)}_{1}]\ \text{ and }\ \overline{O}^{(j)}=(v^{(j)}_{n_{j}},+\infty).

With these notations, we define discrete signed measures

\mu_{L,U}
and a constant
b
as follows.
For
L,U\subseteq[d]
with
0<|L|+|U|\leq s
, let

\mu_{L,U}
be the discrete signed measure
supported on the lattice (
17
), defined by

\displaystyle\mu_{L,U}\big(\{((v^{(j)}_{p_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{p_{j}+1})/2,j\in L)\times((v^{(j)}_{q_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{q_{j}+1})/2,j\in U)\}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}:\widetilde{L}\supseteq L,\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s\end{subarray}}\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in L}I^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}I^{(j)}_{q_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

for
(p_{j},j\in L)\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}-1]
and

(q_{j},j\in U)\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}-1]
.
Also, let

b=c+\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\nu_{L,U}\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big).

By construction, for each
(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}[n_{j}]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}\big(v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\geq l_{j}\big)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}\big(v^{(j)}_{m_{j}}<u_{j}\big)\,d\mu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{r}}\in\prod\limits_{j\in L}\{1,\dots,m_{j}-1\}\times\prod\limits_{j\in U}\{m_{j},\dots,n_{j}-1\}}\mu_{L,U}\big(\{((v^{(j)}_{r_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{r_{j}+1})/2,j\in L)\times((v^{(j)}_{r_{j}}+v^{(j)}_{r_{j}+1})/2,j\in U)\}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}:\widetilde{L}\supseteq L,\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s\end{subarray}}\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big).

It follows that for each

(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}[n_{j}]
,

\displaystyle f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}(v^{(1)}_{m_{1}},\dots,v^{(d)}_{m_{d}})=b+\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}:\widetilde{L}\supseteq L,\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s\end{subarray}}\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle=c+\sum_{0<|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\sum_{L\subseteq\widetilde{L}}\sum_{U\subseteq\widetilde{U}}\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle=c+\sum_{0<|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}\Big(\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}}\big(\underline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\cup\underline{O}^{(j)}\big)\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}}\big(\overline{I}^{(j)}_{m_{j}}\cup\overline{O}^{(j)}\big)\Big)={f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(v^{(1)}_{m_{1}},\dots,v^{(d)}_{m_{d}}),

which implies that
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}
agrees with

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
at all design points

{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}|\mu_{L,U}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|})\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in\prod_{j\in L}[n_{j}-1]}\sum_{{\mathbf{q}}\in\prod_{j\in U}[n_{j}-1]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}:\widetilde{L}\supseteq L,\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle|\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L}I^{(j)}_{p_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}I^{(j)}_{q_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}:\widetilde{L}\supseteq L,\widetilde{U}\supseteq U\\
|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{I}^{(j)}_{n_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{I}^{(j)}_{1}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0<|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}\sum_{L\subseteq\widetilde{L}}\sum_{U\subseteq\widetilde{U}}|\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in L}\underline{I}^{(j)}_{n_{j}}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}\setminus L}\underline{O}^{(j)}\times\prod_{j\in U}\overline{I}^{(j)}_{1}\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}\setminus U}\overline{O}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}|\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}|\Big(\prod_{j\in\widetilde{L}}\big(\underline{I}^{(j)}_{n_{j}}\cup\underline{O}^{(j)}\big)\times\prod_{j\in\widetilde{U}}\big(\overline{I}^{(j)}_{1}\cup\overline{O}^{(j)}\big)\Big)\leq\sum_{0<|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|\leq s}|\nu_{\widetilde{L},\widetilde{U}}|({\mathbb{R}}^{|\widetilde{L}|+|\widetilde{U}|}).

This proves that
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}
is the desired function
satisfying the conditions of the lemma.
∎

A.3
Proofs of Theorems, Lemma, and Corollary in Section
5

A.3.1
Proof of Theorem
4

We will use the following three results from empirical process theory to prove
the theorem. Theorem
8
provides a moment inequality
for the expected supremum of multiplier empirical processes.
Lemma
7
bounds the expected supremum of
empirical processes with Rademacher multipliers in terms of bracketing entropy
integrals. Theorem
9
reduces the problem of
controlling the expected supremum of general multiplier empirical processes to
the case with Rademacher multipliers. While Theorems
8
and

9
are general results,
Lemma
7
is more specific to our setting.
We provide the proof of Lemma
7
in Appendix

A.5.1
.

Theorem 8

(Proposition 3.1 of
16
)
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{F}}
is a countable collection of functions from
\mathcal{X}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Assume that
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
are i.i.d. with law
P

on
\mathcal{X}
and that
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
are independent mean-zero random
variables, independent of
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
. Then,
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C^{p}\bigg[{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]^{p}+p^{p/2}n^{p/2}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\|f\|_{P,2}\Big)^{p}\cdot\max_{i}\|\xi_{i}\|_{2}^{p}

\displaystyle+p^{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}\Big(|\xi_{i}|^{p}\cdot\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}|f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})|^{p}\Big)\Big]\bigg]

for every
p\geq 1
. Here,
\|\cdot\|_{P,2}
is defined by

\|f\|_{P,2}=\big({\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}[f^{2}(X)]\big)^{1/2}.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(k)}
are i.i.d. random
variables on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with density
p_{0}
, and let

\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{k}
be independent Rademacher random variables,
independent of
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(k)}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a
countable collection of functions from
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
, and suppose there
exist
t,D>0
such that
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t
and

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq D
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
. Then,

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(1+D\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{k}}\Big),

where
C
is a universal constant, and

J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
is the bracketing entropy integral defined by

J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon,

with
N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
denoting the

\epsilon
-bracketing number of
{\mathcal{F}}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}
.

Theorem 9

(Corollary 1 of
19
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
be countable collections of functions from

\mathcal{X}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
such that
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}\supseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
for every

1\leq k\leq n
. Suppose
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}

are permutation invariant random variables on
\mathcal{X}
, and let

\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
be i.i.d. mean-zero random variables,
independent of
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
. Assume that
there exist
p\geq 1
and
C>0
such that

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq Ck^{1/p}

for every
1\leq k\leq n
, where
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{n}
are
independent Rademacher random variables, independent of

{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)}
.
Then, for every
q\geq 1
,

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq 4C\|\xi_{1}\|_{\min(p,q),1}\cdot k^{1/\min(p,q)},

where for each
r\geq 1
,

\|\xi_{1}\|_{r,1}:=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(|\xi_{1}|>t)^{1/r}\,dt.

Remark 3
.

The function classes in the above results are assumed to be countable
to ensure measurability of the suprema inside the expectations. For an
uncountable function class
{\mathcal{F}}
and a stochastic process

(\Phi(f):f\in{\mathcal{F}})
indexed by
{\mathcal{F}}
, the supremum

\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Phi(f)
may not be measurable.

In the proof of Theorem
4
, to avoid such a
measurability issue, we define the expected supremum of

\Phi
over
{\mathcal{F}}
as

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Phi(f)\Big]:=\sup\Big\{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{G}}}\Phi(f)\Big]:{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}\text{ is countable}\Big\},

following
29
.
Similarly, for any
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we define

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Phi(f)\geq c\Big):=\sup\Big\{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{G}}}\Phi(f)>c\Big):{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}\text{ is countable}\Big\}.

With these definitions, we can avoid measurability concerns, and
the above theorems and lemma also extend to uncountable function classes.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V)
denote the collection of all functions

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
of the form
(
7
) satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

\nu_{L,U}
are supported on

\prod_{j\in L}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]\times\prod_{j\in U}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]

(b)

\sum_{L,U:0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V.

It is clear from the definition of
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
that

{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V)\subseteq\{f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}:{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\leq V\}.

Also, the following lemma, proved in
Appendix
A.5.2
, guarantees the existence of

f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V)
such that
f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}(\cdot)=f_{0}(\cdot)
on

\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
.

Lemma 8
.

For every
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
with
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)<V
, there exists

f_{{\mathbf{M}}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V)
such that
f_{{\mathbf{M}}}(\cdot)=f(\cdot)
on

\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
.

For each
t>0
, define

B(V,t)=\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V):\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\}.

We suppress the dependence of
B(V,t)
on

{{\mathbf{M}}}=(M_{1},\dots,M_{d})
for brevity. The following lemma,
proved in Appendix
A.5.3
,
provides a bracketing entropy integral bound for
B(V,t)
,
which will play a crucial role throughout the proof.

Lemma 9
.

There exists a constant
C_{B,s}>0
,
depending on
B
and
s
, such that for all
t>0
,

J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{B,s}d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg(t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg).

Now, suppose we have
t_{n}>4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}
such that for every
r\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}/(V+1),

(49)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}/(V+1),\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}/(V+1),

where
\epsilon_{i}
are Rademacher random variables
independent of
{\mathbf{x}}^{(i)}
, and the expectations are taken over

{\mathbf{x}}^{(i)}
,
\epsilon_{i}
, and
\xi_{i}
.
In what follows, we will first see how these bounds on the expected suprema can
be used to obtain a risk bound for
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
. The value of
t_{n}
satisfying the
above inequalities will be specified in the next step, after which
more precise risk bounds will be derived. The subscript
n

emphasizes that
t_{n}
depends on
n
, while its dependence on other parameters
is suppressed for notational simplicity.

We first aim to bound
{\mathbb{P}}(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>t)
for
t\geq t_{n}
.
Fix
r\geq 1
, and for each integer
j\geq 2
, define

{\mathcal{F}}_{j}=\big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V):2^{j-2}rt_{n}<\|f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}<2^{j}rt_{n}\big\}.

By construction,

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>rt_{n}\big)={\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}>rt_{n}\big)\leq\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\big).

Next, let
(M_{n}(f):f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V))
denote the stochastic processes defined by

M_{n}(f)=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f^{*})^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})

and define
(M(f):f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V))
by

M(f)=-\|f-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}.

Since

M_{n}(f)=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f^{*})^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2},

we have

M_{n}({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}})-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\geq 0,

as
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
minimizes the least squares over
{\mathcal{F}}_{{\mathbf{M}}}(V)
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})

and

M(f)-M(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})=-\|f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}-2{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\big[(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}})\big].

The assumption
t_{n}>4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}=4\|f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}
implies that for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
,

\displaystyle-(M(f)-M(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}))

\displaystyle\geq\|f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}\cdot\big(\|f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}-2\|f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{j-2}rt_{n}\cdot\Big(2^{j-2}rt_{n}-\frac{t_{n}}{2}\Big)\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}.

Therefore,
2
2

2

Because of our definitions in Remark
3
,
introduced to avoid measurability issues, some additional care is required in
justifying the first inequality. A more detailed argument is provided in
the remark following the proof.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\big)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\big(M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)\geq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\big((M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}))-(M(f)-M(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}))\big)\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\big[(f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})({\mathbf{x}})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}})\big]\Big|\geq 2^{2j-8}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

(50)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\big[f({\mathbf{x}})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}})\big]\Big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big),

where the second inequality uses that

-(M(f)-M(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}))\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
,
and the last inequality follows because

f-f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
.

We next bound each term on the right-hand side of (
A.3.1
).
As a preliminary step, we show that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C(V+t)
for every
f\in B(V,t)
.
Suppose
f\in B(V,t)
is of the form

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=c+\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

where

\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V.

Since the sum of the total variations of the signed measures is bounded by
V
,
the second term in the above representation of
f
is uniformly bounded in
absolute value by
V
.
Hence, by Cauchy inequality,

t^{2}\geq\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}=\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}})\cdot p_{0}({\mathbf{x}})\,d{\mathbf{x}}\geq\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}\Big(\frac{c^{2}}{2}-V^{2}\Big)\cdot p_{0}({\mathbf{x}})\,d{\mathbf{x}}=\frac{c^{2}}{2}-V^{2}.

It follows that

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq|c|+V\leq C(V+t)

for some universal constant
C>0
.

We now bound the first term on the right-hand side of (
A.3.1
).
By Markov’s inequality,

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2^{6j-21}r^{6}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big].

To bound the expectation on the right, we apply Theorem
8
,
which gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]^{3}+C\cdot 2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+Cn^{-3/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}\Big(|\xi_{i}|^{3}\cdot\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}|f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})|^{3}\Big)\Big].

(51)

Using (
49
), the inequality

\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\xi_{i}|^{3},

and the preliminary result

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})\ \text{ for all }f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n}),

(52)

we deduce from (
A.3.1
) that

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big]\leq C\cdot 2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}+C\cdot 2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+Cn^{-1/2}\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}(V^{3}+2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}).

Substituting this back into the Markov inequality bound, we obtain

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{C}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{2}t_{n}^{6}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{2}t_{n}^{3}}.

We next bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
A.3.1
).
We divide into two cases depending on whether
2^{j}rt_{n}\leq V
or not.
First, suppose
2^{j}rt_{n}\leq V
.
By Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

(53)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{6j-21}r^{6}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|^{3}\Big].

Also, by the standard argument of symmetrization (see, e.g.,

35
and

33
),

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|^{3}\Big]\leq 8{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big],

(54)

where
\epsilon_{i}
are Rademacher random variables independent of

{\mathbf{x}}^{(i)}
.
Applying Theorem
8
, we can bound the expectation on
the right-hand side as

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big]\leq C\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]^{3}

\displaystyle+C\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\|f^{2}\|_{p_{0},2}\Big)^{3}+Cn^{-3/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}|f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})|^{6}\Big].

The contraction principle (see, e.g.,

35
and

22
), together with
(
49
) and
(
52
), gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})\cdot 2^{j}r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}/V.

Moreover, we have

\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\|f^{2}\|_{p_{0},2}\leq\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\|f\|_{\infty}\cdot\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})\cdot 2^{j}rt_{n}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}\cdot 2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}/V^{3}+C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}\cdot 2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}+Cn^{-3/2}(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{6}.

Combining this with (
53
) and
(
54
) yields

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}V^{3}}+\frac{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{6}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}\leq\frac{C}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+\frac{CV^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{CV^{6}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}.

Next, assume that
2^{j}rt_{n}>V
. For each
f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})
, as seen
in the preliminary step, we can decompose
f
as
f=c+g
where
c
is a
constant with
|c|\leq C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})
and
g
is a function uniformly
bounded in absolute value by
2V
. Using this decomposition, we can write

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)=2c\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|g\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big).

It follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\qquad\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)\Big|\geq\frac{2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}}{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big)\\
&\qquad\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|g\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big).\end{split}

(55)

By Markov’s inequality, the first term of
(
A.3.1
) is bounded as

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)\Big|\geq\frac{2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}}{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big)

(56)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}}{2^{6j-24}r^{6}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)\Big|^{3}\Big].

Also, by the standard argument of symmetrization,

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)\Big|^{3}\Big]\leq 8{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big].

(57)

Using Theorem
8
, the expectation on the right-hand
side can be bounded as

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\Big]\leq C\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]^{3}+CV^{3}.

(58)

Applying Lemma
7
and
Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]={\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in B(2V,2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(2V,B(2V,2V),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(1+2V\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(2V,B(2V,2V),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{4V^{2}\sqrt{n}}\Big)\leq C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}V,

where
a_{d,s}:=d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}
.
Combining this with (
