56
),
(
57
), and
(
58
) yields

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim p_{0}}[g(X)]\big)\Big|\geq\frac{2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}}{C(V+2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big)\leq\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}(V+2^{j}rt_{n})^{3}}{2^{6j-24}r^{6}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}}\leq\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}},

where the last inequality follows from the assumption that
2^{j}rt_{n}>V
.
By a similar argument, the second term in
(
A.3.1
) is bounded by

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in F_{{\mathbf{M}}}(2V)\\
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2V\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(g^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|g\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{6}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{6}}\leq\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}.

Substituting these bounds back into (
A.3.1
)
gives

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}.

Thus, whether or not
2^{j}rt_{n}\leq V
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f^{2}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-\|f\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-7}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)\leq\frac{C}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{CV^{6}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}.

The third term on the right-hand side of (
A.3.1
) can
be bounded similarly to the second term in the case
2^{j}rt_{n}\leq V
.
Applying Markov’s inequality, the symmetrization argument, and
Theorem
8
in turn yields

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in B(2V,2^{j}rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\big[f({\mathbf{x}})\cdot(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}})\big]\Big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-8}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+\frac{C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3}V^{3}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}
denotes the supremum norm over

\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]

\|g\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}:=\sup_{{\mathbf{x}}\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}|g({\mathbf{x}})|.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>rt_{n}\big)=\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=2}^{\infty}\Big[\frac{C}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+\frac{C(\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{2}t_{n}^{6}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{2}t_{n}^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{CV^{3}(V^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{r^{3}}+\frac{C(\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{r^{3}n^{3/2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{r^{6}n^{2}t_{n}^{6}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{r^{3}n^{2}t_{n}^{3}}+\frac{CV^{3}(V^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}.

Plugging in
r=t/t_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq\frac{Ct_{n}^{3}}{t^{3}}+\frac{C(\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{n^{2}t^{6}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{n^{2}t^{3}}+\frac{CV^{3}(V^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}},

which holds for all
t\geq t_{n}
.
Thus, for every
t\geq 2t_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>t-\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}\big)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>\frac{t}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ct_{n}^{3}}{t^{3}}+\frac{C(\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{n^{3/2}t^{3}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{n^{2}t^{6}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{n^{2}t^{3}}+\frac{CV^{3}(V^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}},

where the second inequality follows from the assumption

t_{n}>4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}
. Since

\int_{a}^{\infty}2y\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(Y\geq y)dy={\mathbb{E}}[(Y^{2}-a^{2})_{+}],

where
(\cdot)_{+}
denotes the positive part, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 4t_{n}^{2}+\int_{2t_{n}}^{\infty}2t\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\,dt

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leq Ct_{n}^{2}+\frac{C(\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{6}V^{3}}{n^{3/2}t_{n}}+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{n^{2}t_{n}^{4}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}}{n^{2}t_{n}}+\frac{CV^{3}(V^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{3})}{n^{3}t_{n}^{4}}.\end{split}

(59)

We have just seen that once we establish the bounds
(
49
) on the expected suprema with some

t_{n}>4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}
,
we can bound the risk of
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
in terms of
t_{n}
as in the above display.
Our next goal is therefore to identify a suitable
t_{n}
satisfying
(
49
).
To this end, we first bound

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

for each
t>0
and
k=1,\dots,n
, and then apply
Theorem
9
to transfer this bound to the expected
supremum with
\xi_{i}
’s.

Fix
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.
Since
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq C(V+t)
for every
f\in B(V,t)
,
Lemma
7
gives

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(1+C(V+t)\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{k}}\Big).

Applying the entropy integral bound from
Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq C_{B,s}a_{d,s}\bigg(t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg[1+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}(V+t)k^{-1/2}t^{-2}\bigg(t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{B,s}a_{d,s}t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}k^{-1/2}V^{3/2}t^{-1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}k^{-1/2}V^{2}t^{-1}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)}+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}k^{-1/2}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}k^{-1/2}V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}}+C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}k^{-1/2}V\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\max(2,2(\widebar{s}-1))}.

(60)

Recall that
a_{d,s}=d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}
.
Let
\Psi:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
denote the function given by the right-hand side of
(
A.3.1
).
A direct calculation shows that if

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\geq\max\Big(C_{B,s}a_{d,s}(V+1)k^{-1/2}\log(2+k),C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2/3}(V+1)k^{-1/3}[\log(2+k)]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3},

\displaystyle C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{4/5}(V+1)k^{-2/5}[\log(2+k)]^{2\widebar{s}/5},C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}(V+1)k^{-1}[\log(2+k)]^{2},

\displaystyle C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{4/3}(V+1)k^{-2/3}[\log(2+k)]^{2\widebar{s}/3},C_{B,s}a_{d,s}(V+1)k^{-1/2}[\log(2+k)]^{\max(1,\widebar{s}-1)}\Big),

then

\Psi(t)\leq\sqrt{k}t^{2}/(V+1).

To simplify this maximum, observe that for suitable constants
C,C_{s}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\log(2+x)\leq x^{1/6}\ \text{ for all }x\geq C,

\displaystyle[\log(2+x)]^{2\widebar{s}/5}\leq x^{1/15}\ \text{ for all }x\geq C_{s},

\displaystyle[\log(2+x)]^{2}\leq x^{2/3}\ \text{ for all }x\geq C,

\displaystyle[\log(2+x)]^{2\widebar{s}/3}\leq x^{1/3}\ \text{ for all }x\geq C_{s},\text{ and}

\displaystyle[\log(2+x)]^{\max(1,\widebar{s}-1)}\leq x^{1/6}\ \text{ for all }x\geq C_{s}.

Using these inequalities, we can bound terms in the maximum as follows:

\displaystyle k^{-1/2}\log(2+k)\leq Ck^{-1/2}+k^{-1/2}k^{1/6}\leq Ck^{-1/3},

\displaystyle k^{-2/5}[\log(2+k)]^{2\widebar{s}/5}\leq C_{s}k^{-2/5}+k^{-2/5}k^{1/15}\leq C_{s}k^{-1/3},

\displaystyle k^{-1}[\log(2+k)]^{2}\leq Ck^{-1}+k^{-1}k^{2/3}\leq Ck^{-1/3},

\displaystyle k^{-2/3}[\log(2+k)]^{2\widebar{s}/3}\leq C_{s}k^{-2/3}+k^{-2/3}k^{1/3}\leq C_{s}k^{-1/3},\text{ and}

\displaystyle k^{-1/2}[\log(2+k)]^{\max(1,\widebar{s}-1)}\leq C_{s}k^{-1/2}+k^{-1/2}k^{1/6}\leq C_{s}k^{-1/3}.

Thus, if we set

\widetilde{t}_{k}=C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}(V+1)k^{-1/3}[\log(2+n)]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3},

then we have

\Psi(\widetilde{t}_{k})\leq\sqrt{k}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{2}/(V+1).

Since the map
t\mapsto\Psi(t)/t
is decreasing, it follows that

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,r\widetilde{t}_{k})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq\Psi(r\widetilde{t}_{k})\leq r\Psi(\widetilde{t}_{k})\leq r\sqrt{k}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{2}/(V+1)

for every
r\geq 1
.

We have just shown that for each
k=1,\dots,n
,

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,r\widetilde{t}_{k})}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq rk\widetilde{t}_{k}^{2}/(V+1)

for every
r\geq 1
. By Theorem
9
, this bound
transfers to the expected supremum with
\xi_{i}
, giving

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,r\widetilde{t}_{n})}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq 4\|\xi_{1}\|_{3,1}\cdot rn\widetilde{t}_{n}^{2}/(V+1)

for every
r\geq 1
.
Therefore, redefining
\widetilde{t}_{n}
by multiplying it by the factor
(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{3,1})

yields the first two inequalities in (
49
)
(with
t_{n}=\widetilde{t}_{n}
) for
r\geq 1
.

We now bound

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

for each
t>0
. By following the proof of Lemma
7

with minimal modifications, we can show that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,t)}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}\cdot J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\Big(1+C(V+t)\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\Big).

Hence, repeating the computations above, we find that if we define

\widebar{t}_{n}
as

\widebar{t}_{n}=(1+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}})\cdot C_{B,s}a_{d,s}^{2}(V+1)n^{-1/3}[\log(2+n)]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3},

then

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,\widebar{t}_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq\sqrt{n}\widebar{t}_{n}^{2}/(V+1),

from which the last inequality in (
49
) (with
t_{n}=\widebar{t}_{n}
)
follows for all
r\geq 1
.

Using
\widetilde{t}_{n}
and
\widebar{t}_{n}
, we define
t_{n}
as

t_{n}=4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}+\max(\widetilde{t}_{n},\widebar{t}_{n}).

Then, for every
r\geq 1
,

{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in B(V,rt_{n})}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq(rt_{n}/\widetilde{t}_{n})\cdot\sqrt{n}\widetilde{t}_{n}^{2}/(V+1)\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}/(V+1)

and the remaining two inequalities in (
49
)
follow by the same argument. Hence,
t_{n}
satisfies all inequalities in
(
49
) for all
r\geq 1
, and we use this
t_{n}

to derive a risk bound for
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
.

As a last step, we substitute our
t_{n}
into (
A.3.1
).
This yields

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}\big[\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big]\leq C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}+a_{d,s}^{4}(V+1)^{2}\Big[C_{B,s}(1+\|\xi_{1}\|_{3,1}^{2}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}^{2})

\displaystyle\cdot n^{-2/3}[\log(2+n)]^{4(\widebar{s}-1)/3}+O(n^{-2/3})\Big],

when
s\geq 2
. On the other hand, if
s=1
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\|{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}+a_{d,1}^{4}(V+1)^{2}\Big[C\Big(\frac{\max(\widetilde{t}_{n},\widebar{t}_{n})}{a_{d,1}^{2}(V+1)}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\|\xi_{1}\|_{3}^{3}V^{3}}{a_{d,1}^{4}(V+1)^{2}n^{2}(\max(\widetilde{t}_{n},\widebar{t}_{n}))^{4}}+o(n^{-2/3})\Big]

\displaystyle=C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}+a_{d,1}^{4}(V+1)^{2}\cdot O(n^{-2/3}),

where the constant factors underlying
O(\cdot)

depend on
B,s
, the moments of
\xi_{i}
, and

\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty,{\mathbf{M}}}
.
∎

Remark 4
.

For the first inequality in (
A.3.1
), we in fact need to
show that

{\mathbb{P}}\big({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\big)\leq\sup\Big\{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{G}}}\big(M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)\geq 0\Big):{\mathcal{G}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\text{ is countable}\Big\},

since
{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
may not be countable.
Here, we give a more careful argument for this.

For each integer
N\geq 1
, let
{\mathcal{G}}_{N}
denote the subcollection of
{\mathcal{F}}_{j}

consisting of all
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
(of the
form (
7
)) that additionally satisfy the following two conditions:

(a)

\nu_{L,U}
are supported on

\prod_{j\in L}((1/N)\mathbb{Z}\cap(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2])\times\prod_{j\in U}((1/N)\mathbb{Z}\cap(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2])
, where

(1/N)\mathbb{Z}:=\{m/N:m\in\mathbb{Z}\}

(b)

c\in\mathbb{Q}
and
\nu_{L,U}(\{(p_{j},j\in L)\times(q_{j},j\in U)\})\in\mathbb{Q}

for every
(p_{j},j\in L)\times(q_{j},j\in U)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}
.

Clearly, each
{\mathcal{G}}_{N}
is countable, and thus,
{\mathcal{G}}:=\cup_{N\geq 1}{\mathcal{G}}_{N}
is
countable as well. Since
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
is constructed from discrete signed
measures with finite support,
it can be easily shown that there
exists a sequence
\{g_{N}\}_{N\geq 1}
with
g_{N}\in{\mathcal{G}}_{N}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}
such
that
g_{N}({\mathbf{x}})\to{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}({\mathbf{x}})
as
N\to\infty
for every

{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Hence, if
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
, then

\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{G}}}\big(M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)\geq\lim_{N\to\infty}\big(M_{n}(g_{N})-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)=M_{n}({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}})-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\geq 0.

Consequently,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big({\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{G}}}\big(M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)\geq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\Big\{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}}\big(M_{n}(f)-M_{n}(f_{0,{\mathbf{M}}})\big)\geq 0\Big):\mathcal{H}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{j}\text{ is countable}\Big\}.

A.3.2
Proof of Lemma
2

Proof of Lemma
2
.

For each
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in B(V,t)
, by modifying each
basis function
b^{L,U}_{{\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}}
as in
(
40
),
we can express
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
as

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d}):=b+\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(l_{j},j\in S)

(61)

for some
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
and finite signed Borel measures
\mu_{S}

on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|S|}
, related to the original measures
\nu_{L,U}

through (
42
).
Here, the summation runs over all nonempty subsets

S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
.
Since each
\nu_{L,U}
is supported on
\prod_{j\in L}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]\times\prod_{j\in U}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
, the relation
(
42
) implies that each
\mu_{S}
is
supported on
\prod_{j\in S}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
.
Moreover, by (
A.1.5
),

\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq\min(2^{s}-1,2^{d})\cdot\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq(2^{s}-1)V\leq C_{s}V.

Hence, if we define
\widetilde{B}(V,t)
as the collection of all
functions
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
with

\|f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t
such that
\mu_{S}
are supported
on
\prod_{j\in S}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
and satisfy

\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V,

then we have
B(V,t)\subseteq\widetilde{B}(C_{s}V,t)
.

We now split
\widetilde{B}(V,t)
into pieces, compute the bracketing entropy
of each piece, and then put them
together to obtain a bracketing entropy bound for
\widetilde{B}(V,t)
,
which will in turn yield a bound for the bracketing entropy of
B(V,t)
.
For every
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}\in\widetilde{B}(V,t)
, by repeating the argument
(using Cauchy inequality) in the proof of Theorem
4
,
we can show that
|b|\leq C(V+t)
for some constant
C>0
.
Set
K=\lfloor C(V+t)/\epsilon\rfloor
, and for each
k=-(K+1),\dots,K
,
let
{\mathcal{G}}_{k}
denote the collection of all functions

f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{S}\}}
of the form (
61
)
with
k\epsilon\leq b\leq(k+1)\epsilon
and with signed Borel measures

\mu_{S}
supported on
\prod_{j\in S}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
and satisfying

\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V
.
It is clear that

\widetilde{B}(V,t)\subseteq\bigcup_{k=-(K+1),\dots,K}{\mathcal{G}}_{k},

and hence,

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\widetilde{B}(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\log\Big(\sum_{k=-(K+1),\dots,K}N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}_{k},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\Big)

(62)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\log\Big(2+\frac{C(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)+\sup_{k}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}_{k},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\log\Big(2+\frac{C(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}).

Now, let
{\mathcal{G}}_{\emptyset}
denote the collection of all constant functions
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with values in
[0,\epsilon]
.
Also, for each nonempty
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
, define

{\mathcal{G}}_{S}
as the collection of all functions on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\mapsto\int_{\prod_{j\in S}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(l_{j},j\in S),

where
\mu_{S}
is a finite signed Borel measure
on
\prod_{j\in S}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
with
\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V
.
By construction,

{\mathcal{G}}_{0}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{\emptyset}+\bigplus_{0<|S|\leq s}{\mathcal{G}}_{S},

where
A+B=\{a+b:a\in A,b\in B\}
.
It follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}_{0},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{s}(1+\log d)+\sum_{0<|S|\leq s}\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\epsilon/(C_{s}d^{\widebar{s}}),{\mathcal{G}}_{S},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\big),

(63)

where
\widebar{s}=\min(s,d)
.

To proceed, for each nonempty
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
, let

\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{S}
denote the collection of all functions on the
truncated section
\prod_{j\in S}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
of the original
domain
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, obtained by restricting to the coordinates indexed by

S
, of the form

(x_{j},j\in S)\mapsto\int_{\prod_{j\in S}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(l_{j},j\in S),

where
\mu_{S}
is as in the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}_{S}
.
Since
p_{0}
is uniformly bounded by
B/\prod_{j=1}^{d}M_{j}
, we have

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}}_{S},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq N_{[\ ]}\Big(\Big(\frac{\prod_{j\in S}M_{j}}{B}\Big)^{1/2}\epsilon,\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{S},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(64)

Furthermore, for each nonempty
S\subseteq[d]
with
|S|\leq s
, let

\widebar{{\mathcal{G}}}_{S}
denote the collection of all functions on the scaled domain

[0,1]^{|S|}
of the form

(x_{j},j\in S)\mapsto\int_{[0,1]^{|S|}}\prod_{j\in S}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\,d\mu_{S}(l_{j},j\in S),

where
\mu_{S}
is a finite signed Borel measure on
[0,1]^{|S|}
with

\|\mu_{S}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V
. Through a straightforward scaling
argument, it can be readily verified that

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\widetilde{{\mathcal{G}}}_{S},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq N_{[\ ]}\Big(\Big(\frac{1}{\prod_{j\in S}M_{j}}\Big)^{1/2}\epsilon,\widebar{{\mathcal{G}}}_{S},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)=N_{[\ ]}\Big(\Big(\frac{1}{\prod_{j\in S}M_{j}}\Big)^{1/2}\epsilon,\widebar{{\mathcal{G}}}_{[|S|]},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(65)

Next, for each integer
m\geq 1
and
R>0
, let
\mathcal{H}_{m}(R)
denote the
collection of all functions on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\mapsto\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\,d\mu(l_{1},\dots,l_{m})

where
\mu
is a finite Borel measure (not a signed measure) on
[0,1]^{m}
with

\|\mu\|_{\text{TV}}\leq R
. It was proved in
15

that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{H}_{m}(R),\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq C_{m}\Big(2+\frac{R}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{R}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2(m-1)}.

By the Jordan decomposition of signed measures,

\widebar{{\mathcal{G}}}_{[|S|]}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{|S|}(V)-\mathcal{H}_{|S|}(V),

where
A-B=\{a-b:a\in A,b\in B\}
. It follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\widebar{{\mathcal{G}}}_{[|S|]},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq 2\log N_{[\ ]}\big(\frac{\epsilon}{2},\mathcal{H}_{|S|}(V),\|\cdot\|_{2}\big)\leq C_{|S|}\Big(2+\frac{2V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{2V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2(|S|-1)}.

Substituting this bound back into (
65
),
(
64
), (
63
), and
(
62
) in turn, we obtain

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\widetilde{B}(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\log\Big(2+\frac{C(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{B,s}d^{2\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{2(\widebar{s}-1)}\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)}.

Lastly, since
B(V,t)\subseteq\widetilde{B}(C_{s}V,t)
, we arrive at

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{s}(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{B,s}d^{2\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{2(\widebar{s}-1)}\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)},

which completes the proof.
∎

A.3.3
Proof of Corollary
1

Proof of Corollary
1
.

Observe from the proof of Theorem
4
that
the risk bound for
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
depends continuously on
V
.
Consequently, the desired bound in the corollary follows directly
from the risk bound for
{\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
and the following inequality:

{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\leq\liminf_{\epsilon\to 0+}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}\\
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f^{*})\leq V\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V+\epsilon}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}.

∎

A.3.4
Proof of Theorem
5

Our proof of Theorem
5
builds on the proof ideas of

12
, which itself is motivated by the
ideas in
4
. As in
12
,
we use Assouad’s lemma in the following form.

Lemma 10

(Lemma 24.3 of
36

and Lemma 11.20 of
21
)
.

Suppose
q
is a positive integer, and we have
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
with

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}})\leq V
for each
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
. Then, we have
the following lower bound for the minimax risk
{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
:

{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\geq\frac{q}{8}\cdot\min_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\neq{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})}\cdot\min_{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})=1}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}})\big]}\bigg).

Here,
H(\cdot,\cdot)
denotes the Hamming distance

H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}):=\sum_{j=1}^{q}1\{\eta_{j}\neq\eta^{\prime}_{j}\}
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{f}
represents the probability distribution of
(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})
given

({\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(n)})
when
f^{*}=f
, and
K(\cdot,\cdot)

denotes the Kullback divergence between two probability distributions.

Proof of Theorem
5
.

Fix an integer
l
as

l=\bigg\lceil\frac{1}{3\log 2}\Big\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)-(\widebar{s}-1)\log\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\rceil

where
C_{B,\widebar{s}}=B2^{-4\widebar{s}+1}(6\log 2)^{\widebar{s}-1}\cdot(\widebar{s}-1)!
and

\lceil x\rceil
denotes the smallest integer greater than or equal to
x
.
This choice of
l
ensures that

2^{-l}\leq\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}\Big)^{1/3}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{(\widebar{s}-1)/3}<2^{-l+1}.

(66)

Define

P_{l}=\bigg\{(p_{1},\dots,p_{\widebar{s}})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{\widebar{s}}:\sum_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}p_{j}=l\bigg\},

and, for each
{\mathbf{p}}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{\widebar{s}})\in P_{l}
, let

I_{{\mathbf{p}}}=\big\{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\widebar{s}}):i_{j}\in[2^{p_{j}}]\mbox{ for each }j\in[\widebar{s}]\big\}.

Recall that
[m]=\{1,\dots,m\}
for each integer
m\geq 1
. It is
clear that
|I_{{\mathbf{p}}}|=2^{l}
for every
{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}
, and

|P_{l}|=\binom{\widebar{s}+l-1}{\widebar{s}-1}\geq\frac{l^{\widebar{s}-1}}{(\widebar{s}-1)!}.

Next, define

Q=\big\{({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}):{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}\mbox{ and }{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}\big\}

and let
q=|Q|=|P_{l}|\cdot 2^{l}
.
In this proof, functions will be indexed by vectors
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
,
whose components are indexed by the set
Q
.

For an integer
m\geq 1
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, denote by
\psi_{m,k}
the
real-valued function on
(0,1)
defined by

\displaystyle\psi_{m,k}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if }x\in\big((k-1)2^{-m},(k-3/4)2^{-m}\big)\cup\big((k-1/4)2^{-m},k2^{-m}\big),\\
-1&\mbox{if }x\in\big((k-3/4)2^{-m},(k-1/4)2^{-m}\big),\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Using these functions
\psi_{m,k}
, we construct
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}

for
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
as follows, with which we will use Lemma

10

to prove the lower bound of the minimax risk
{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
. For each

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, let
\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}
be the signed Borel measure on

\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2)
defined by

d\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}({\mathbf{t}})=\frac{1}{M_{1}\cdots M_{\widebar{s}}}\cdot\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}\Big(\frac{t_{j}}{M_{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\bigg)\,d{\mathbf{t}},

and define
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
as

f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2)}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\,d\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}({\mathbf{t}}).

Clearly,
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
for every
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
The following lemma, whose proof is deferred to
Appendix
A.5.4
, summarizes the key properties of the
functions
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}
we need for the proof.

Lemma 11
.

For each
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, the complexity of
f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}
is
bounded by
V
, i.e.,

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f_{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\leq V.

(67)

We also have

\max_{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})=1}\|f_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\leq\frac{BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-4\widebar{s}+2}

(68)

and

\min_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\neq{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})}\geq\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-6\widebar{s}+2}.

(69)

It is straightforward to check that the Kullback divergence between

{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}}
for

{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
can be computed by

K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}})=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\big)^{2}.

Hence, (
68
) gives

\max_{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})=1}{\mathbb{E}}\big[K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}})\big]=\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}}\cdot\max_{H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})=1}\|f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\leq\frac{BnV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-4\widebar{s}+1}.

(70)

Applying Lemma
10
, along with
(
69
) and (
70
), we can bound
the minimax risk
{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}
as

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{q}{8}\cdot\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-6\widebar{s}+2}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{BnV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-4\widebar{s}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq bV^{2}2^{-2l-6\widebar{s}-1}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2(6\log 2)^{\widebar{s}-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-3l}}{l^{\widebar{s}-1}}}\bigg).

Recall that
q=|P_{l}|\cdot 2^{l}
,

|P_{l}|\geq l^{\widebar{s}-1}/(\widebar{s}-1)!
, and

C_{B,\widebar{s}}=B2^{-4\widebar{s}+1}(6\log 2)^{\widebar{s}-1}\cdot(\widebar{s}-1)!
.
Our choice of
l
(at the beginning of the proof) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(6\log 2)^{\widebar{s}-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-3l}}{l^{\widebar{s}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Bigg[\frac{\log\big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}{2\Big(\log\big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)-(\widebar{s}-1)\log\log\big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)\Big)}\Bigg]^{\widebar{s}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\bigg[2\bigg(1-(\widebar{s}-1)\frac{\log\log\big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}\bigg)\bigg]^{-(\widebar{s}-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\bigg[2\bigg(1-(\widebar{s}-1)\Big\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\Big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]^{-(\widebar{s}-1)}.

Here, the first inequality follows from (
66
) and

l\geq\frac{1}{3\log 2}\bigg\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)-(\widebar{s}-1)\log\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg\},

and the last inequality is from the inequality

\log\log x/\log x\leq(\log x)^{-1/2}
, which is valid for all

x>1
. If we assume that

n\geq\frac{e^{4\widebar{s}^{2}}}{C_{B,\widebar{s}}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}},

then

\frac{1}{2(6\log 2)^{\widebar{s}-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-3l}}{l^{\widebar{s}-1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Big(1+\frac{1}{\widebar{s}}\Big)^{-(\widebar{s}-1)}\leq\frac{1}{2},

and thereby, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\cdot bV^{2}2^{-2l-6\widebar{s}-1}\geq\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\cdot bV^{2}2^{-6\widebar{s}-3}\cdot\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}\Big)^{2/3}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3}

\displaystyle\geq C_{b,B,\widebar{s}}\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}V}{n}\Big)^{2/3}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3},

where
C_{b,B,\widebar{s}}=(1-1/\sqrt{2})\cdot b2^{-6\widebar{s}-3}C_{B,\widebar{s}}^{-2/3}
.
Here, (
66
) is used again for the second inequality.
Lastly, since

\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,\widebar{s}}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\Big(\frac{nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

provided that
n\geq(1/C_{B,\widebar{s}}^{2})\cdot(\sigma^{2}/V^{2})
,
by further assuming that

n\geq\max\Big\{\frac{e^{4\widebar{s}^{2}}}{C_{B,\widebar{s}}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}},\frac{1}{C_{B,\widebar{s}}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}}\Big\},

we can derive the lower bound

{\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}}\geq C_{b,B,\widebar{s}}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}V}{n}\Big)^{2/3}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{2(\widebar{s}-1)/3}

where
C_{b,B,\widebar{s}}^{\prime}=C_{b,B,\widebar{s}}\cdot 2^{-2(\widebar{s}-1)/3}
.
∎

A.4
Proofs of Proposition and Lemma in Section
6

A.4.1
Proof of Proposition
7

Proof of Proposition
7
.

Suppose
f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
for

{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
and

\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\equiv f
.
By repeating the argument in the proof of
Proposition
5
, it can be shown that
for every
g\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
,

{\widetilde{V}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(g)\leq V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(g)={\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(g),

where
V_{{\mathbf{a}}}(\cdot)
is defined as in (
44
).
Applying this inequality to each
f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
yields

\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}})\geq\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\widetilde{V}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}})\geq{\widetilde{V}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f),

which proves one direction of the desired identity:

{\widetilde{V}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)\leq\inf\Big\{\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}}):\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\equiv f,\ f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}\ \forall{\mathbf{a}}\Big\}.

We now turn to the reverse inequality. Suppose

f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
is expressed as

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=c+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

for some
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
and signed Borel measures

\nu_{L,U}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}
. For each

{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
, define

f_{{\mathbf{a}}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\displaystyle f_{{\mathbf{a}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=c\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}).

It is clear that
f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
for all

{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
.
Moreover, since for each integral over
\nu_{L,U}
,
there is exactly one value of
{\mathbf{a}}
that makes all multiplied
indicator functions equal to one, we have

\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\equiv f.

Fix
{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
. For each nonempty

S\subseteq[d]
, we have

\displaystyle f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)=c\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{d}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U\subseteq S:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}}).

Hence, for each nonempty
T\subseteq[d]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U\subseteq S:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\Big(\sum_{S:L\cup U\subseteq S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\Big)\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset,L\cup U=T\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{|L|+|U|}}\prod_{j\in L}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq l_{j})\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}<u_{j})\,d\nu_{L,U}({\mathbf{l}},{\mathbf{u}})

if
|T|\leq s
, and it vanishes otherwise.
For each nonempty
T\subseteq[d]
with
|T|\leq s
, since there is at
most one pair of
(L,U)
with
L\cap U=\emptyset
and

L\cup U=T
such that

\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)=1,

by repeating the computation in the proof of
Proposition
5
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})

\displaystyle={\text{Vit}}\Big((x_{j},j\in T)\mapsto\sum_{S\subseteq T}(-1)^{|T|-|S|}\cdot f^{S}_{(a_{j},j\in S^{c})}(x_{j},j\in S)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset,L\cup U=T\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)\cdot\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<|T|\leq d}{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})=\sum_{0<|T|\leq s}{\text{Vit}}(f^{T}_{(a_{j},j\in T^{c})})

\displaystyle=\sum_{0<|T|\leq s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset,L\cup U=T\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)\cdot\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)\cdot\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Summing the above identity over all

{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}
gives

\displaystyle\inf\Big\{\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}}):\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\equiv f,\ f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}\ \forall{\mathbf{a}}\Big\}\leq\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}\Big(\prod_{j\in U^{c}}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=-\infty)\cdot\prod_{j\in U}{\mathbf{1}}(a_{j}=+\infty)\Big)\cdot\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L,U:L\cap U=\emptyset\\
0<|L|+|U|\leq s\end{subarray}}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Taking the infimum over all possible representations

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
of
f
, we arrive at

\inf\Big\{\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}{\text{HK}}_{{\mathbf{a}}}(f_{{\mathbf{a}}}):\sum_{{\mathbf{a}}\in\{-\infty,+\infty\}^{d}}f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\equiv f,\ f_{{\mathbf{a}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}\ \forall{\mathbf{a}}\Big\}\leq{\widetilde{V}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f),

which completes the proof.
∎

A.4.2
Proof of Lemma
3

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Suppose that
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\text{ST}}}
and that
f=\sum_{k=1}^{K}f_{k}
, where
each
f_{k}
is a regression tree with right-continuous splits and
depth at most
s
. Let
{\mathbf{w}}_{k}
denote the leaf-weight vector of
f_{k}
.

Fix an integer
L\geq 1
. For each
k
, define the regression tree

g_{k,L}:=(1/L)f_{k}
, obtained by scaling each leaf weight of
f_{k}

by
1/L
while keeping the same tree structure. Using these
g_{k,L}
,
we can represent
f
as a sum of
KL
trees:

f=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{L}g_{k,L}.

For this representation, the sum of the
p^{\text{th}}
powers of the
leaf weights equals

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\|(1/L)\cdot{\mathbf{w}}_{k}\|_{p}^{p}=\frac{1}{L^{p-1}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{k}\|_{p}^{p}.

Because
p>1
, this quantity converges to
0
as
L\to\infty
.
This proves that
{V^{d,s}_{\text{XGB}}}(f;p)=0
.
∎

A.5
Proofs of Lemmas in Appendix
A.3

A.5.1
Proof of Lemma
7

Lemma
7
is a corollary of the following
more general result involving Bernstein norm. For a random variable
X

with law
P
on
\mathcal{X}
and a function
f:\mathcal{X}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
the Bernstein norm of
f
is defined by

\|f\|_{P,B}=\Big(2{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\big[\exp(|f(X)|)-1-|f(X)|\big]\Big)^{1/2}.

Although
\|\cdot\|_{P,B}
does not satisfy homogeneity or the triangle
inequality and therefore is not actually a norm, it is conventionally called a
norm and can still be used for measuring the “size” of functions.

Lemma 12

(Lemma 3.4.3 of
35
)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{x}}^{(1)},\dots,{\mathbf{x}}^{(k)}
are i.i.d. with law
P

on
\mathcal{X}
and
{\mathcal{F}}
is a countable collection of functions from

\mathcal{X}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
where
\|f\|_{P,B}\leq\delta
for all
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(\delta,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})\cdot\Big(1+\frac{J_{[\ ]}(\delta,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{k}}\Big).

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Let
P
be the law of
({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)},\epsilon_{i})
on

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}
and let
{\mathcal{G}}
denote the collection of all functions

\Phi_{f}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}
, one for each
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
, defined by

\Phi_{f}({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)=\frac{\epsilon f({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\quad\text{for }({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}.

For every
\Phi_{f}\in{\mathcal{G}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{f}\|_{P,B}

\displaystyle=\bigg(2{\mathbb{E}}_{({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)\sim P}\Big[\exp\Big(\Big|\frac{\epsilon f({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|\Big)-1-\Big|\frac{\epsilon f({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|\Big]\bigg)^{1/2}=\bigg(2\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\Big[\Big|\frac{f({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|^{m}\Big]\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(2\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\Big[\Big|\frac{f({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|^{2}\Big]\bigg)^{1/2}\leq\frac{(e-2)^{1/2}t}{2^{1/2}D}:=\frac{at}{2D},

where the first inequality uses the fact that
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq D
,
and the second inequality follows from the fact that
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t
.
Applying Lemma
12
with
{\mathcal{G}}
and

\delta=at/2D
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle=2D\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sup_{\Phi_{f}\in{\mathcal{G}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Phi_{f}({\mathbf{x}}^{(i)},\epsilon_{i})\Big|\Big]

(71)

\displaystyle\leq 2D\cdot CJ_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{at}{2D},{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)\cdot\Big(1+\frac{J_{[\ ]}(\frac{at}{2D},{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}{(\frac{at}{2D})^{2}\sqrt{k}}\Big).

Next, we relate the bracketing entropy integral of
{\mathcal{G}}
in the Bernstein norm
to that of
{\mathcal{F}}
in the
\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}
norm.
Fix
\Phi_{f}\in{\mathcal{G}}
, and let
[f_{1},f_{2}]
be a bracket containing
f
.
Since
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq D
,
by replacing
f_{1}
with
{\mathbf{x}}\mapsto\max(\min(f_{1}({\mathbf{x}}),D),-D)

if necessary, we assume that
\|f_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq D
.
Similarly, we assume that
\|f_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq D
.
Define
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\Phi_{1}({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)=f_{1}({\mathbf{x}})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}(\epsilon=1)-f_{2}({\mathbf{x}})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}(\epsilon=-1)\quad\text{for }({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}

and

\Phi_{2}({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)=f_{2}({\mathbf{x}})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}(\epsilon=1)-f_{1}({\mathbf{x}})\cdot{\mathbf{1}}(\epsilon=-1)\quad\text{for }({\mathbf{x}},\epsilon)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{-1,1\}.

Clearly, the bracket
[\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}]
contains
\Phi_{f}
.
Moreover, since
\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 2D
, by the same argument as
above, we obtain

\|\Phi_{2}-\Phi_{1}\|_{P,B}=\bigg(2{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}\sim p_{0}}\Big[\exp\Big(\Big|\frac{(f_{2}-f_{1})({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|\Big)-1-\Big|\frac{(f_{2}-f_{1})({\mathbf{x}})}{2D}\Big|\Big]\bigg)^{1/2}\leq\frac{a}{2D}\cdot\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{p_{0},2}.

It follows that

N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{a\epsilon}{2D},{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)\leq N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\quad\text{for }\epsilon>0.

Hence,

\displaystyle J_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{at}{2D},{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{at/2D}\sqrt{1+N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}\,d\epsilon=\frac{a}{2D}\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{a\epsilon}{2D},{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{2D}\cdot J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}).

Substituting this bound into (
71
)
completes the proof.
∎

A.5.2
Proof of Lemma
8

We use the following lemma, which ensures that the supports of the
signed measures can be restricted to

\prod_{j\in L}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]\times\prod_{j\in U}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]

without changing the function on
\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
.
The proof is omitted, as it can be proved similarly to
Lemma
6
.

Lemma 13
.

For every
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}
, there
exists
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}
with
finite signed Borel measures
\mu_{L,U}

supported on
\prod_{j\in L}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]\times\prod_{j\in U}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
such that

(a)

f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}(\cdot)={f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(\cdot)

on
\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]

(b)

\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}.

Proof of Lemma
8
.

Suppose
f\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
with
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f)<V
. By the definition of
the complexity measure
{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(\cdot)
, there exists

{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\in{\mathcal{F}^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{ST}}}
such that
{f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}\equiv f
and

\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V.

Lemma
13
also guarantees the
existence of
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}
with finite signed Borel measures

\mu_{L,U}
supported on
\prod_{j\in L}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]\times\prod_{j\in U}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
satisfying conditions (a) and (b)
of the lemma.
By condition (a),
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}(\cdot)={f^{d,s}_{c,\{\nu_{L,U}\}}}(\cdot)=f(\cdot)
on

\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]
.
Moreover, by condition (b),

\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\mu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq\sum_{0<|L|+|U|\leq s}\|\nu_{L,U}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq V.

Hence,
f^{d,s}_{b,\{\mu_{L,U}\}}
is a desired function satisfying
the conditions of Lemma
8
.
∎

A.5.3
Proof of Lemma
9

Proof of Lemma
9
.

We use the bracketing entropy bound for
B(V,t)
established in
Lemma
2
, which gives

\displaystyle J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq t+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{s}(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}\,d\epsilon+C_{B,s}d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\,d\epsilon.

To handle the integrals on the right-hand side, we use the following lemma,
which is a straightforward consequence of integration by parts (see, e.g.,

21
).

Lemma 14
.

For
u>t
,

\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\log\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}\,d\epsilon=\frac{t}{2\sqrt{\tau}}\cdot(1+2\tau)

and

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{k}\,d\epsilon\leq C_{k}u^{1/2}t^{1/2}(1+\tau^{k}),

where
\tau=\log(u/t)
and
C_{k}
is a constant depending on
k
.

By the first inequality in Lemma
14
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{C_{s}(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}\,d\epsilon\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\log\Big(\frac{2t+C_{s}(V+t)}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{1/2}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq Ct\Big[1+2\log\Big(\frac{2t+C_{s}(V+t)}{t}\Big)\Big]\leq C_{s}t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big).

Also, by the second inequality in Lemma
14
and the
inequality
(x+y)^{1/2}\leq x^{1/2}+y^{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\,d\epsilon\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{2t+V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(\frac{2t+V}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}(2t+V)^{1/2}t^{1/2}\bigg(1+\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg)\leq C_{s}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}+C_{s}V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}.

Combining these bounds yields

\displaystyle J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{s}t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+C_{B,s}d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}

\displaystyle+C_{B,s}d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}.

If
t\leq V
,

t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\leq V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1},

and otherwise,

t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\leq C_{s}t\leq C_{s}t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big).

Hence, in both cases, we have

J_{[\ ]}(t,B(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{B,s}d^{\widebar{s}}(1+\log d)^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg(t\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)+V^{1/2}t^{1/2}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{\widebar{s}-1}\bigg).

∎

A.5.4
Proof of Lemma
11

Proof of (
67
).

By definition, for each
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, we have

\displaystyle|\nu_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}|\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\Big(-\frac{M_{j}}{2},\frac{M_{j}}{2}\Big)\bigg)=\frac{1}{M_{1}\cdots M_{\widebar{s}}}\cdot\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2)}\bigg|\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}\Big(\frac{t_{j}}{M_{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\bigg|\,d{\mathbf{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\int_{(0,1)^{\widebar{s}}}\bigg|\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg|\,d{\mathbf{t}}\leq\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\int_{(0,1)^{\widebar{s}}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{t}}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\int_{(0,1)^{\widebar{s}}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{t}}\Bigg)^{1/2}=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\int_{(0,1)^{\widebar{s}}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{t}}\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\int_{0}^{1}(\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j}))^{2}\,dt_{j}\bigg)^{1/2}=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\bigg(\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}2^{-p_{j}}\bigg)^{1/2}=V.

Here, the inequality is from Cauchy inequality, the third equality
follows from the fact that

\int_{0}^{1}\psi_{m,k}(x)\psi_{m^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(x)\,dx=0

for distinct
m
and
m^{\prime}
, and the fourth equality is due to that

\psi_{m,k}\psi_{m,k^{\prime}}\equiv 0
provided
k\neq k^{\prime}
. This proves that

{V^{d,s}_{\infty-\text{XGB}}}(f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}})\leq V
for every
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
∎

Proof of (
68
).

For an integer
m\geq 1
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, let
\Psi_{m,k}
be the
real-valued function on
[0,1]
defined by

\Psi_{m,k}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\psi_{m,k}(t)\,dt.

It can be readily verified that

\displaystyle\text{(i) }\Psi_{m,k}(x)=0\ \text{ if }x\leq(k-1)2^{-m}\text{ or }x\geq k2^{-m}

(72)

\displaystyle\text{(ii) }\Psi_{m,k}(x+2^{-m-1})=-\Psi_{m,k}(x)\ \text{ for }x\in[(k-1)2^{-m},(k-1/2)2^{-m}]

\displaystyle\text{(iii) }|\Psi_{m,k}(x)|\leq 2^{-m-2}\ \text{ for all }x\in[0,1].

Also, for every
(x_{1},\dots,x_{\widebar{s}})\in[0,1]^{\widebar{s}}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\Big(M_{1}x_{1}-\frac{M_{1}}{2},\dots,M_{\widebar{s}}x_{\widebar{s}}-\frac{M_{\widebar{s}}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{M_{1}\cdots M_{\widebar{s}}}\cdot\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}(-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2)}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\Big[{\mathbf{1}}\Big(M_{j}x_{j}-\frac{M_{j}}{2}\geq t_{j}\Big)\cdot\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}\Big(\frac{t_{j}}{M_{j}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\Big]\,d{\mathbf{t}}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\cdot\int_{(0,1)^{\widebar{s}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\big[{\mathbf{1}}(x_{j}\geq t_{j})\cdot\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\big]\,d{\mathbf{t}}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\int_{0}^{x_{j}}\psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\,dt_{j}=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

(73)

We prove (
68
) using equation
(
A.5.4
). Assume that we are given

{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
with
H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime})=1
and
that
({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})
is a unique element in
Q
for which

\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\neq\eta^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}
.
We then have

(f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}})\Big(M_{1}x_{1}-\frac{M_{1}}{2},\dots,M_{\widebar{s}}x_{\widebar{s}}-\frac{M_{\widebar{s}}}{2}\Big)=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\cdot(\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}-\eta^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}})\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}),

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\|f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B}{M_{1}\cdots M_{d}}\cdot\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}\big((f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{\widebar{s}})\big)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle=B\cdot\int_{[0,1]^{\widebar{s}}}\Big((f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}})\Big(M_{1}x_{1}-\frac{M_{1}}{2},\dots,M_{\widebar{s}}x_{\widebar{s}}-\frac{M_{\widebar{s}}}{2}\Big)\Big)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\int_{0}^{1}\big(\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j})\big)^{2}\,dx_{j}\leq\frac{4BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}2^{-p_{j}}\cdot 2^{-2p_{j}-4}=\frac{BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-4\widebar{s}+2}.

Recall that
B=M_{1}\cdots M_{d}\cdot\sup_{{\mathbf{x}}}p_{0}({\mathbf{x}})

for the first inequality.
∎

Proof of (
69
).

Fix
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}\neq{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
For an integer
m\geq 1
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, let
h_{m,k}
be the
real-valued function on
[0,1]
defined by

h_{m,k}(x)=\begin{cases}2^{m/2}&\mbox{if }(k-1)2^{-m}<x<\big(k-1/2\big)2^{-m},\\
-2^{m/2}&\mbox{if }\big(k-1/2\big)2^{-m}<x<k2^{-m},\\
0&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

and, for each
({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})\in Q
, let

H_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}
be the real-valued function on

[0,1]^{\widebar{s}}
defined by

H_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\widebar{s}})=\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}h_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

It can be readily checked that

\{H_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}:({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})\in Q\}
is an
orthonormal set in
L^{2}([0,1]^{\widebar{s}})
.
Consider the function
g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}:[0,1]^{\widebar{s}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

defined by

g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{\widebar{s}})=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\big(\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}-\eta^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\big)\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

Since

b=M_{1}\cdots M_{d}\cdot\inf_{{\mathbf{x}}\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}p_{0}({\mathbf{x}})>0,

we have

\displaystyle\|f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{M_{1}\cdots M_{d}}\cdot\int_{\prod_{j=1}^{d}[-M_{j}/2,M_{j}/2]}\big((f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{\widebar{s}})\big)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle=b\cdot\int_{[0,1]^{\widebar{s}}}\Big((f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}})\Big(M_{1}x_{1}-\frac{M_{1}}{2},\dots,M_{\widebar{s}}x_{\widebar{s}}-\frac{M_{\widebar{s}}}{2}\Big)\Big)^{2}\,d{\mathbf{x}}=b\|g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2},

where
\|\cdot\|_{2}
denotes the
L^{2}
norm.
Recall (
A.5.4
) in the proof of
(
68
) for the last equality. By Bessel’s
inequality, it thus follows that

\|f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq b\|g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq b\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}^{\prime}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}^{\prime}}}\langle g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}},H_{{\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}}\rangle^{2},

(74)

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the
L^{2}
inner product.

Observe that for each
({\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime})\in Q
,

\langle g_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}},H_{{\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime}}\rangle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\cdot\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}\big(\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}-\eta^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}\big)\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\langle\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle.

(75)

We conclude the proof by showing that for

({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}),({\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime})\in Q
,

\prod_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}\langle\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=\begin{cases}2^{-3l/2-3\widebar{s}}&\mbox{if }({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})=({\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime})\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(76)

Once (
76
) is proved, by combining it with
(
74
) and (
75
),
we can derive that

\|f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}-f_{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-6\widebar{s}}\sum_{{\mathbf{p}}\in P_{l}}\sum_{{\mathbf{i}}\in I_{{\mathbf{p}}}}(\eta_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}}-\eta^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}}})^{2}=\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-3l-6\widebar{s}+2}\cdot H({\boldsymbol{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}),

from which (
69
) directly follows.
We first consider the case where

({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})\neq({\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime})
.
If
{\mathbf{p}}\neq\mathbf{p^{\prime}}
, then,
since
\sum_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}p_{j}=l=\sum_{j=1}^{\widebar{s}}p^{\prime}_{j}
, there exists
j\in[\widebar{s}]

such that
p_{j}>p^{\prime}_{j}
. In this case,
h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}
is constant
on
((i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}},i_{j}2^{-p_{j}})
, and hence,
(
72
) implies that

\langle\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=0
. If

{\mathbf{p}}=\mathbf{p^{\prime}}
, then
i
and
i^{\prime}
must be distinct, and
thus, there exists
j\in[\widebar{s}]
such that
i_{j}\neq i^{\prime}_{j}
. In
this case,
\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)\cdot h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}(x)=0
for all

x\in[0,1]
, and clearly,

\langle\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=0
. For the case where

({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{i}})=({\mathbf{p}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{i}}^{\prime})
,
(
76
) follows from the fact that

\langle\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p_{j},i_{j}}\rangle=\int_{(i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}}}^{i_{j}2^{-p_{j}}}\Psi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)\cdot h_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)\,dx=2^{-3p_{j}/2-3}

for each
j\in[\widebar{s}]
.
∎
